PR #55 (RS-A-filtername) merged as 822ec28 at 2026-11-13 13:40:53Z, through auto-merge (set at 13:33:26Z, once the pushed head bee2ded showed its 9 jobs; all 9 passed), under the user's delegation of 2026-11-11 (CONFIRMED here from `gh pr view`). Its round 5, on cae155e..bee2ded, has no section of its own, since nothing was committed after it:

- Opus on the test: nothing CONFIRMED, INFERRED or HYPOTHESIZED wrong. Held (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): `DROP_ENV` widened by the numbered keys and values from 1 up, by the keys alone, by the values alone, by one number (50), by `KEY_100` alone, by two digits or more and by three digits or more each fails the real-repos test; four digits or more, 101 to 999 and a leading zero pass (above the range the comment gives, and git reads no name with a leading zero); with cae155e's test the change from 1 up passes; with 11391c1's `gitbin.js` the real-repos test fails.
- Sonnet on the docs: nothing WRONG. Three points under "could be clearer", none in bee2ded. Two are taken here, each read against the code first: MANUAL.md's `odd-filter-name` row says where to rename a filter that a variable hands to git, and `docs/how-it-works.md` names settings handed to git in the environment among those whose filters keep running (`USER_SCOPES` has `command`; a setting from `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` or `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` shows the scope `command`, CONFIRMED here with raw git, 08:46). The third is left: the row does not say why an attributes line that is only `filter` skips status as well. It already says the two cannot be told apart, and more text in that row costs more than it gives (DEFERRED, RS-D-barefilter; repro: the row, MANUAL.md section 16).
- Both clones had an empty `git status --short` and an empty `tmp-review` (checked here before they were removed).

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED by Fable in round 3 of PR #55 and here on 69d4eb4 (`probe-redirect.js`, section "RS-A-filtername round 3" below): Git for Windows reads `GIT_REDIRECT_STDIN`, `GIT_REDIRECT_STDOUT` and `GIT_REDIRECT_STDERR`. With the first naming an empty file, `git check-attr --stdin` read that file in place of the paths it was sent and answered nothing, `filterOverrides` took that for "no file has a filter", and `git status` ran the repo's filter. With the third as `2>&1` and an index extension git skips, the warning landed in front of the first path `git ls-files` printed, and that file's filter ran. With the second, status was not read at all (`outside-worktree`).
- The class (CONFIRMED here in round 3, `scan-wide-names.js`): the installed `git.exe` 2.56.1 holds exactly three names that start with `GIT_` as 16-bit text, these three. Not gone through: names that do not start with `GIT_`, and the wrapper `cmd/git.exe`.
- Fix, both copies, two parts that each hold alone:
  - `gitbin.js`: `DROP_ENV` drops the three names, in any capitals, so no git the plugin starts gets them (`gitEnv` is the environment of every such call).
  - `gitfacts.js`: `filterOverrides` takes the answer of check-attr only when it is `<path>\0filter\0<value>\0` for each path sent, in the order sent, and ends with its mark. Anything else gives `filter-check`, and status is not read. This part does not know why an answer is wrong, so it also covers a cause nobody has listed. In the stderr case as run, check-attr prints its own warning in front of its answer, so the answer is not about the path sent and this part refuses it too (round 1 below; this note first said the opposite, from reasoning and not from a run). A warning that only ls-files printed would pass it.
- That check-attr answers so for every path (CONFIRMED here with raw git 2.56.1 on Windows and on WSL, `probe-attr-echo.js`, 08:35): one answer for each path `git ls-files -z` prints, in order, with the same text, for 27 odd names on Windows and 36 on Linux (spaces, a leading dash, quotes, a tab, a line break and a backslash on Linux, letters outside ASCII), for a file in a conflict (listed three times, answered three times), for 3000 files, and for a tracked file gone from disk. Not run on an older git: a git that answers otherwise would now give `filter-check` in every repo with a tracked file, and CI's three systems show theirs.
- Tests, `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies, three named "(RS-A-redirect)":
  - The decision, with git stood in for (the stand-in holds both questions and check-attr's input): a whole answer is read and the filter switched off; a path listed three times and answered three times is read; 19 wrong answers are refused with `filter-check` and nothing asked after them (empty; the first, the middle or the last path left out; another order; another path, other capitals, `./` in front, a space after; an answer twice in place of the next; an answer more; another word than `filter`, and `FILTER`; no end mark; a mark more; a mark in front; a value left out; a line in front of the first path; a line after the last mark). Not told: right paths in the right order with other values than git's.
  - `gitEnv`: each of the three names in capitals, in small letters and in a mix is dropped, all three together too, and 12 names one step away stay.
  - Real repos: for five rows of variables (the first, the first in small letters, the third with the index extension, the second, all three with the extension), a repo with a filter `evil` is read with 1 counted and 1 filter switched off, a repo with a filter named `set` gives `odd-filter-name`, no marker is written, and the file `GIT_REDIRECT_STDOUT` names is not made. Then git itself is run in each repo and must write the marker. On Windows the test first asks raw git under `GIT_REDIRECT_STDIN` and must get an empty answer, so the rows can fail there; with any other git they hold only that the answers are right.
- On the parent's `gitbin.js` and `gitfacts.js` the three tests fail (CONFIRMED here, `repro-redirect-parent.js`, 08:50), each for the fault: the decision test on "an empty answer" (taken, where `filter-check` is meant), the `gitEnv` test with `GIT_REDIRECT_STDIN` kept, the real-repos test with 2 counted and both markers written under `GIT_REDIRECT_STDERR`. That run is the reproduction.
- Mutations (`mutate-redirect.js`, each through `dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-redirect'`, 18 probes, one whole run after the last change to the code and the tests, CONFIRMED here, kept as `mutate-redirect-1.txt`, 08:45 to 08:49), none other than listed before the run. Of 6 (three tests in two copies): the parent's two files fail 6. The parent's `gitbin.js` alone, each of the three names out of `DROP_ENV`, and the names dropped in capitals only fail 4 (the `gitEnv` test and the real-repos test). The pattern widened three ways (every name that starts with `GIT_REDIRECT_`, with `GIT_REDIRECT`, a name with more after it) fails 2 (the `gitEnv` test). The parent's `gitfacts.js` alone, the number of answers not checked, the end mark not checked, the path not checked, the word not checked, and a wrong answer read as "no filters" fail 2 (the decision test). Every answer checked against the first path, and the empty entry after the last path kept, fail 4 (the decision test and the real-repos test). The unchanged files pass.
- Linux (WSL Ubuntu, `wsl-redirect-test.sh`), Node 18.19.1, git 2.56.1: the three tests pass, three runs (CONFIRMED here, 08:50, kept as `wsl-redirect-run1.txt`). The variables do nothing in that git, so the real-repos test holds there only that the answers are right.
- Shipped docs, changed with the code: `docs/how-it-works.md` names the three variables among those dropped, says the list of filters is taken only with one answer for each tracked path, and its `filter-check` row says so too; MANUAL.md's `filter-check` row likewise. PR #56 was opened on 2026-11-13 (5eb9170, pushed 08:58). Three reviewers, each in its own clone under `E:/DevTemp` (`rv-redirect-r1-security`, `-regression`, `-docs`). All three clones had an empty `git status --short` and an empty `tmp-review` afterwards (checked here).

Held (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run): the three names in four capital forms with eight values each (96 runs) gave status read and the filter switched off, and neither named file was made; 74 other settings, from the 197 names that start with `GIT_` in git.exe and the names in the cmd wrapper, ran no filter; all four git calls in `scripts/lib` use `gitEnv()` with stderr ignored; each new return is `filter-check` and sets `statusSkipped`. Not settled (HYPOTHESIZED by Fable, not tried): the index changed by another process between check-attr and status. INFERRED by Fable, not run: trace output sent to stdout on Linux (`GIT_TRACE=/dev/stdout`) is refused.
  - One older finding, SERIOUS, DEFERRED and flagged to the user: RS-A-dotpath (CONFIRMED by Fable and here, `probe-dslash.js`, 09:22, the same on 822ec28 and on 5eb9170, git 2.56.1 on Windows; Linux INFERRED by Fable, not run). An index written by hand lists a tracked path with an empty or `.` part (`d//b.bin`, `d/./b.bin`, `d///b.bin`), and an attributes line has the same text with `filter=evil`. `git check-attr` prints the path as sent and answers `unspecified`, `git status` goes by the index's text, and the repo's filter runs during the plugin's status check: the marker is absent after `gitFacts` without `status` and present after it with `status`, with `statusCount` 1 and nothing skipped. The control `d/b.bin` is switched off and does not run. The answer check of this PR takes that answer, since it is whole. Fix to make (INFERRED, Fable): `odd-path` for a tracked path with an empty or `.` part. Not in this PR: older than its diff, and a change to shipped code of its own.
- Opus, regression and tests: two findings, both reproduced here.

### RS-A-redirect round 2 (2026-11-13, on 5eb9170..be6a7d9, PR #56)

be6a7d9 was pushed at 09:56. Two reviewers, since no script changed (no security row): clones `rv-redirect-r2-regression` and `rv-redirect-r2-docs` under `E:/DevTemp`. Both had an empty `git status --short` and an empty `tmp-review` afterwards (checked here).

These passed every test: a path that differs at its second character, or at its last but one; a path in other capitals after an exact first character; a path that is the same after NFKC, or after NFC; a backslash for a slash; a tab for a space; the same characters in another order; and for the word the second character, the capitals, NFKC and the order. The ten texts of be6a7d9 were chosen, one for each loosening thought of.
  - Fix: the test changes shape. `near(t)` is now every text one change away at every place (a character left out, put in or changed, two neighbors swapped), over every ASCII character but NUL and six others (U+00A0, U+0131, U+0130, U+017F, U+212A, U+0301; untrue of DEL at 30656fc, see rounds 3 and 4), with the wide form for a changed character, and five texts further away (`./` in front, all capitals, all wide forms, a backslash for each slash, no text). A floor holds that the loops ran (more than 250 texts for each character). Two more cases: a path with U+00E9 is read as itself and refused as `e` with U+0301. Not told, and said so in the comment: a text two or more changes away other than those five. No change to the scripts. After it the whole list ran again (54 changes with the unchanged files last, each through `dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-redirect'`, CONFIRMED here, kept as `mutate-redirect-3b.txt`, 10:10 to 10:24): each change fails as listed before the run and none otherwise, the twelve of this round each failing the decision test alone (2 of 6), and the unchanged files pass. Linux (WSL, Node 18.19.1, git 2.56.1): the three tests pass, three runs, on the test as committed (CONFIRMED here, 10:24, kept as `wsl-redirect-run4.txt`).
  - Held (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): none of be6a7d9's 60 texts equals the right one and each answer is otherwise whole; the new cases fail on 822ec28's `gitfacts.js` (61 of 65, the other 4 being the answers meant to be read); the decision test reads no environment; with real git 2.56.1 on Windows the two warning cases have the shape git prints, and check-attr without the variable echoes a path with a warning in it as a whole answer, as the corrected note says; the notes and the two lessons of round 1 match what was run.
- Sonnet, docs: one WRONG, reproduced here, and three points under "could be clearer".
  - WRONG (CONFIRMED by Sonnet and here with raw git 2.56.1, `probe-redirect-where.js`, 09:58): `docs/how-it-works.md` said the three variables make git "send its output or its warnings to another place than the one the hook reads". With `GIT_REDIRECT_STDERR=2>&1` the warning lands in git's output, in front of the first path, which is what the hook reads. 5eb9170's sentence was untrue in the same way, and be6a7d9 only reworded it. The sentence now names one effect for each variable, each seen in that probe: git reads its input from a file (`GIT_REDIRECT_STDIN=<an empty file>`: check-attr answered nothing), writes its output to a file (`GIT_REDIRECT_STDOUT=<a file>`: the list was in the file and nothing came back), or puts its warnings in with its output. ## Next version: RS-A-filtername (2026-11-12, branch `claude/rs-a-filtername` off 358d3c3, merged in PR #55 as 822ec28)

The user decided on 2026-11-12, asked whether the filter-name fault gets fixed next as its own PR ahead of the other deferred items: "Yes, fix it next". Whether a release follows is the user's call and was not asked yet.

PR #54 (RS-A-filterkey) merged as 8420586 at 2026-11-12 19:05:38Z, through auto-merge (set at 18:48:59Z, once the pushed head 358d3c3 showed its 9 jobs), under the user's delegation of 2026-11-11 (CONFIRMED here from `gh pr view`). Two things about its last commit, 358d3c3, that the commit itself does not say:

- It lacks the line with its quick check's reading: `--sums --quick` exit 0, observed 13:48, the handoff-checks step 610 s at 33 to 43% load (35% in the middle), `SHA256SUMS` unchanged. The commit went out in the same message as the edit that was to add the line, and the edit failed (lesson logged).
- It had no model review. It changes one sentence of these notes and no row of the review table applies to `dev/`.

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 358d3c3 and on the installed copy of 0.6.2 (`probe-filtername.js`, with `echo ran >> <marker>; cat` as the command), and by Fable in round 1 of PR #54: `git check-attr -z` prints `set`, `unset`, `unspecified` or an empty text for a file with no filter, and the same text for a file whose filter has that word, or nothing, as its name (`*.bin filter=set`, `*.bin filter=`). `filterOverrides` in `gitfacts.js` took the four texts for "no filter" and went on. With `[filter "set"]` (or one of the other three names) and a `clean` command in the repo's own `.git/config`, the status call got no `-c filter.<name>.clean=` and the command ran when a hook read `git status`. The same line is in v0.5.0 and v0.6.2. What it needs: a folder that came with its own `.git/config` (an unpacked archive, a copied folder); a clone does not carry one.
- The class (PLAYBOOK "Writing code and docs" step 11): a text from git that stands for two things. By grep over `scripts/` in both copies:
  - `filterOverrides` is the only place that reads values of `check-attr`, and `gitFacts` (one call, for `status`) is its only caller.
  - `git status` is the only git command the code runs that runs filters.
  - The four texts are the whole list of what git prints for an attribute that has no value of its own (git's page on `check-attr`, read here in the installed copy: `unspecified`, `unset`, `set`, else the value; that a value may be empty is from the probe, `*.bin filter=`). The page itself ends its examples with "Not all values are equally unambiguous" and an attribute whose value is the word `unspecified`.
  - Any other text is a filter's name and was handled already: a plain name is looked up and switched off when the repo's config has a setting for it, another name is `odd-filter-name`.
- Fix, `gitfacts.js`, both copies. The text cannot be told apart, so the code asks who could be meant. `filterOverrides` notes which of the four texts tracked files got. If any, one more call (`git config -z --get-regexp '^filter\.(set|unset|unspecified)?\.'`) lists the settings of filters under the four names in every config git reads. A setting of a filter whose name is one of the texts seen gives `odd-filter-name`, and status is not read. Exit status 1 from the call means no such setting. Any other failure gives `filter-check`, or `git-timeout` for a timeout. `filterOverrides` is exported, for its test.
- What that costs a user: a filter so named in the user's own global or system config skips status too (the call does not ask for scopes), and a filter named `unspecified` does it in nearly every repo, since nearly every repo has a file with no filter. Renaming the filter brings status back. Kept this way on purpose: telling the scopes apart would leave the repo's own filter of that name to be found by a second rule, and no tool known here names a filter so (INFERRED: Git LFS uses `lfs`, and git's page on attributes uses `indent`, `crypt` and `p4`, read here in the installed copy; not searched further).
- After, CONFIRMED here (`probe-filtername.js` on the fixed tree): `odd-filter-name` for each of the four names, the marker not written, and the control filter `evil` switched off with 1 counted.
- Test, `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies: "git status: a filter named as git names "no filter" is not left running (RS-A-filtername)". Two layers.
  - The decision, asked of `filterOverrides` with a stand-in for git that answers `ls-files`, `check-attr` and the two config calls and counts the new one. For files that get each of the four texts: skipped with a filter of the same name and read with each of the other three; read with no such filter; skipped when the filter's only setting is `smudge`, `process` or `required`; skipped with that filter before or after one named `lfs`; skipped when the value holds dots and a line break; read when another filter's value holds a line that reads `filter.<name>.clean`; read for seven names near the text (longer, shorter, with a dot, in upper case, `lfs`); read for `filter.lfs.<name>` and for `filter.clean`; skipped among files that have a filter of another name; `git-timeout` when the call timed out; `filter-check` when it failed with status 128 or with no status. With files that all have a filter of another name the call is not made (counted 0).
  - The way there, in real repos with one tracked file `x.bin`, changed and set 5 s newer than the index, and a `clean` command that writes a marker. For each of the four names as the filter's name: `statusSkipped` is `odd-filter-name`, the marker is absent, and then `git status` run by the test itself writes the marker (so the absence means something). `*.bin filter` with a filter named `set`: skipped too. Five forms of the attributes with no such filter: read, 1 counted. `*.bin -filter` with a filter named `set`: read.
  - Its comment says what it does not tell: a filter so named in the user's own config (the same call; the repos set their own config only), and what any other answer of `check-attr` means.
- On 358d3c3's `gitfacts.js`, with only the export added so that the test finds the function, the test fails 2 of 2 (CONFIRMED here: the first probe of the mutation list). That run is the reproduction.
- Mutations (`mutate-filtername.js`, each through `dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-filtername'`, 23 probes, one whole run after the test's last change, CONFIRMED here, kept as `mutate-filtername-run1.txt`): 22 caught 2 of 2, and the unchanged fix passes. Caught: the parent's file; the table without each of its four texts, one at a time; a text of the table not noted; the call's pattern without each of the four names, one at a time; the call made only when a file has a filter of another name; the call made after the answer for no filter at all; a failed call counted as no such filter; a failed call never a timeout; a failed call always a timeout; exit status 1 counted as a failure; any filter under one of the four names, seen or not; any setting of any filter; a filter so named found and status read all the same; a setting read with its value; a filter with no name not seen; a name taken up to its first dot.
- Linux without git-lfs (WSL Ubuntu, Node 18.19.1, git 2.56.1, a copy of `plugins/trailmark`, `wsl-filtername-test.sh`): the test passes 3 of 3 (CONFIRMED here, kept as `wsl-filtername-run1.txt`). So git on Linux runs such a filter too, and the marker is written there.
- The neighbours: `dev/release-check.sh --only 'git status'` gave 16 of 26 passed and 10 skipped, exit 0, 122 s (CONFIRMED here, observed 14:12). The 10 skips are 5 tests in each copy that need symlinks or a file system that keeps raw bytes in names (read in the kept TAP output). The two long suites did not run here; CI runs them.
- The cost, a library timing in this repo (134 tracked files, Windows, `time-filtername-call.js`): the added call alone took 61 to 111 ms over 12 runs, 65 in the middle (CONFIRMED here). A whole `gitFacts(dir, ['status'])` took 714 to 947 ms with the fix over 8 runs. No time of the hook as a process was taken on the parent and on the fix: the change adds one start of git and nothing else on that path (INFERRED from the diff), and the status snapshot is read once per compaction.
- Old git: the added call uses `-z` (in `git config` since 1.5.3) and `--get-regexp` (named in the notes of 1.7.7.2 as existing), both read here in the release notes of the installed git. The function already needs `--show-scope` (2.26) once a file has a filter, so the call asks nothing newer. Not run on an old git.
- Shipped docs, changed with the code: the `odd-filter-name` row in MANUAL.md (section 16's table) and in `docs/how-it-works.md`, and one new paragraph in "Filters and `git status`" there. SECURITY.md's sentence on filters ("repo-configured filters" switched off) stands and is unchanged. CHANGELOG.md has no section for unreleased work and gets none here, like the items before.

### RS-A-filtername round 1 (2026-11-12, on 358d3c3..b8efc9c, PR #55)

Fable on the security of `gitfacts.js`, Opus on regressions and the test, Sonnet on the two shipped docs, each from a clone of its own. No reviewer found a way for a folder's own files to get a command run. This batch changes the new call in `gitfacts.js` (one point of Fable's), the test, the two docs and these notes. CI on b8efc9c: 9 of 9 jobs passed (seen in `gh pr view`, 15:27). The Codex bot's code review of b8efc9c reports no findings; its security review says completed and gives no result (read in its comment's state line, 15:27).

- Fixed, Fable 2, exit status 1: the call took exit status 1 for "no such setting". CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: with `core.repositoryformatversion=1` and an unknown `extensions.*` key the same call exits 1 while `[filter "set"]` is defined; `git status` exits 128 there and `gitFacts` answers `git-error` before the call, so nothing runs. CONFIRMED by Fable and here (`probe-killed-status.js`, a node child of the probe's own ended with `taskkill /F`): on Windows `execFileSync` reports such a child as `status=1`, `signal=null`. So a git ended from outside at that call was read as "no such filter". Nothing in a repo's files was found that ends git that way (HYPOTHESIZED reach, Fable).
  - The change: the call brings a setting of its own (`-c trailmark.asked=1`) and its pattern asks for it too (`^(trailmark\.asked$|filter\.(set|unset|unspecified)?\.)`). A whole answer always has that setting and exit status 0 (CONFIRMED here in this repo: exit 0 with the setting, exit 1 without). Any failure, status 1 included, gives `filter-check`, or `git-timeout`, and so does an answer without the setting.
  - A repo that writes `trailmark.asked` in its own config adds a line to the answer and changes nothing: the call's exit status is what says the answer is whole, and the setting is checked on top of it.
  - Deferred, RS-A-exitone (seen on 358d3c3 and b8efc9c, the line `const scoped = sc.ok || sc.status === 1;` in `filterOverrides`): the older call that lists where each filter's settings come from reads exit status 1 the same way. Ended from outside there, git would leave a repo's filter of an ordinary name running (INFERRED from the code, not run). Repro: `probe-killed-status.js` for what Windows reports; the fix is the same setting on that call. Not in this PR: it is older, and no way to it from a repo's files is known.
- Fixed, test gaps (Opus F1 and F2, Fable 1; each CONFIRMED by the reviewer and here, `mutate-filtername.js . 'r1: '` on b8efc9c, kept as `mutate-filtername-r1-before.txt`): the test passed with the call narrowed to `clean` settings, with `--no-includes`, and with `--local`. With the first, a filter named `set` that has only a `process` setting ran; with the others, one in a file the repo's config includes (both by the reviewers' probes in real repos, not re-run). Cause: the test's stand-in for git answered the same whatever it was asked, so the cases for `smudge`, `process` and `required` held the code that reads the answer and not the call; and every real repo defined the filter with `clean` in `.git/config`.
- Fixed, Opus F3 (INFERRED by Opus as outside what the test claims; CONFIRMED here that the change passed, same run): the table of four texts asked as a plain object passed, and would take `constructor` for "no filter". The shipped code has a Set. This is the swap PLAYBOOK "Writing code and docs" step 11 asks for, from the lesson of 2026-11-12 "A mutation list changed numbers and lines, and never the kind of table", and it was left out of this item's first list (lesson logged).
- The test now, in two tests so that a run says which layer caught a change:
  - "..., the decision (RS-A-filtername)". The stand-in checks the call (its own setting, then `config`, `-z`, `--get-regexp`, a pattern, and nothing more) and answers from it: the settings whose key the pattern matches, the call's own setting last, exit status 1 when nothing matches. The cases for names near the four texts run twice, once so and once with the whole text handed over. New cases: exit status 1, an empty answer, and an answer without the call's own setting are each `filter-check`; files whose filter is named `constructor`, `__proto__`, `toString`, `hasOwnProperty` or `valueOf` do not make the call (counted 0).
  - "..., in real repos (RS-A-filtername)". New cases, each `odd-filter-name` (the marker's absence, also claimed here at first, proved something in two of the six only: section "RS-A-filtername round 2"): a filter named `set` with only `process`, only `smudge` or only `required`; under `[filter.set]`; in a file `.git/config` includes; in the worktree's own config (`extensions.worktreeConfig`); and in the user's own config (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, for one call, put back after). The last closes what the first comment said it did not tell.
  - Its comment says what it does not tell: other answers of `check-attr`, attributes from another place than the top `.gitattributes`, and that the cases which expect status read take the machine's own config to have no filter under the four names (Opus, INFERRED, not run).
- Mutations after the batch (`mutate-filtername.js`, each through `dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-filtername'`, the whole list after the last change to the test, CONFIRMED here, kept as `mutate-filtername-run3.txt`): 32 probes, none other than listed before the run.

Fable's times, 8 runs each, alternating: 1023 against 946 ms with 1 file, 1149 against 1005 ms with 2,000 files. That fits the 65 ms measured here for the call alone. The loop over the largest answer the runner lets through (32 MB) took 443 ms and does not look at the clock; kept, since the runner's buffer bounds it.

MANUAL.md's row now says whose config is meant (the repo's or the user's) and that the filter is renamed both in the config and in the attributes file. `docs/how-it-works.md` says "git runs such a filter, so `git status` would run it" for "Such a filter runs", and that the user's global and system config are asked too. ### RS-A-filtername round 2 (2026-11-12, on b8efc9c..11391c1, PR #55)

Fable on the security of `gitfacts.js`, Opus on regressions and the two tests, Sonnet on the two shipped docs, each from a clone of its own. One finding is CONFIRMED in what 11391c1 changed (Fable 1), so this batch changes `gitbin.js` (one word and its comment), the two tests, two docs and these notes, and its diff gets a round 3. CI on 11391c1: 9 of 9 jobs passed (seen in `gh pr view`, 16:04).

- Fixed, Fable 1, `GIT_CONFIG` (CONFIRMED by Fable and here): with `GIT_CONFIG=<file>` in the session's environment, `git config` reads that one file and nothing else, not the repo's config and not `-c`, while `git status` goes by the real configs. git's own page says so ("This variable has no effect on other Git commands", `git-config.html` of git 2.56.1, read here). So the call's own setting, which 11391c1 takes as proof that git answered in full, came from the named file or not at all.
  - Seen here, the batch's cases on 11391c1's code (`dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-filtername'`, kept as `filtername-r2-before.txt`, 2 failed of 4: the real-repos test in both copies). With a file that holds `trailmark.asked`: a repo with a filter named `set` was read (1 counted) and the filter ran; an ordinary filter (`filter=evil`, defined in the repo's config) ran too. With an empty file: `filter-check` for all three repos, the one with no filter among them, so status was read nowhere.
  - Fable 2, older (CONFIRMED by Fable on b8efc9c and 11391c1; here on 11391c1 with the file that holds `trailmark.asked`, same run): under `GIT_CONFIG` the older call that lists where each filter's settings come from sees nothing of the repo's config, so an ordinary filter is not switched off and runs. On 11391c1 with an empty file it does not run, only because the new call fails first.
  - A repo's files cannot set the variable: it needs the user's environment (INFERRED by Fable and here from how hooks get their environment; nothing in a repo was found that sets a variable for the hook).
  - The change: `gitEnv` in `plugins/*/scripts/lib/gitbin.js` drops `GIT_CONFIG` (the word `CONFIG` in `DROP_ENV`, matched whole, any capitals). `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` and `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` stay: they change what every git command reads, so the checks and `git status` still agree under them.
  - Why in this PR and not an item of its own: this PR's check rests on it, and the older half is in code the fix touches (lesson of 2026-11-10, "A better key widened an older gap").
  - The class (lesson of 2026-11-10, "A fix covered the reviewer's example, not its class"): a variable that changes what the checks ask (`ls-files`, `check-attr`, `config`) and not what `git status` does. Raw git 2.56.1 on Windows in a scratch repo (`probe-env-views.js`, CONFIRMED here): of `GIT_CONFIG`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` with a key and value, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`, `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_INDEX_VERSION` and the three `GIT_*_PATHSPECS`, only `GIT_CONFIG` splits the two. `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` moves both together: `check-attr` shows no filter and `git status` runs none. Round 3's security brief asks for the class again, with more variables.
  - The other `git config` calls (grep of `scripts/lib`): the one that reads `.lfsconfig` from the index or HEAD (`config --blob`) failed under `GIT_CONFIG` ("only one config file at a time", exit 129, CONFIRMED here with raw git), which gave `lfs-config` and no status; it now answers as without the variable. The one in `gitbin.js` names its file (`--file`) and was never touched by the variable (CONFIRMED here, raw git).
- Fixed, Fable 3, a test gap (CONFIRMED by Fable; here by the mutation list below): `continue` changed to `break` where the answer's own setting is met passed both tests. With `[trailmark] asked` ahead of `[filter "set"]` in a repo's config, that change reads status and the filter runs. No case had a repo with `trailmark.asked` of its own, and round 1's sentence that such a repo "changes nothing" was read from the code. Cases now: in the decision test, for each of the four names and both ways the stand-in answers, a `trailmark.asked` of the repo's before the filter's setting and after it (skipped) and alone (read); in real repos, `[trailmark] asked = x` ahead of `[filter "set"]` (skipped, marker absent) and alone (read).
- Fixed, Opus F1 (CONFIRMED by Opus; read here in the test): four of round 1's six new real cases said the marker was absent and could not have written it. The cases with only `process` and under `[filter.set]` had no marker command; `smudge` and `required` settings run nothing during `git status`. Now the first two carry the marker command (`process = <the command>`, and `clean = <the command>` under `[filter.set]`), and the last two assert `odd-filter-name` and nothing about a marker. Each of the five cases that has the command (those two, the included file, the worktree's own config, and the one after the repo's own `trailmark.asked`) is now followed by `git status` itself in that repo, which must write the marker: the forced fault for each case, where round 1 had it for the four names only. Round 1's sentence on these cases is corrected to point here.
- Not taken, Fable 4 (INFERRED by Fable, low): require the answer's last entry to be exactly `trailmark.asked` with the value `1`. Exit status 0 is what says git answered in full, and the setting is checked on top of it; with `GIT_CONFIG` dropped no file can supply the setting in place of `-c`. A check on the order would rest on where each git version prints command-line settings.
- Held, by Opus (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): no answer differs between b8efc9c and 11391c1 for the repos of round 1's list. b8efc9c's file fails the decision test on the assertion about the call's shape, not on the fault, and passes the real-repos test (git does not fail there); 358d3c3's file with the export added fails both for the fault. 11 changes of the notes' list and 14 of Opus's own came out as stated. INFERRED by Opus, not run: on a git older than 2.32 the case with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` fails (a failure, never a pass that should not be); a `'` in the temp folder's path fails the test the same way.
- Held, by Fable (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run): without `GIT_CONFIG`, nothing in a repo's files made the call answer "whole, and no such filter" while such a filter was defined: the repo's own `trailmark.asked` in every place and form tried, keys shaped like it, a config that breaks after the filter, a very large answer.
- Docs (Sonnet): nothing wrong. Two points taken: MANUAL.md's row now says that the new name in the config is what brings status back and that the attributes file is renamed so that git keeps applying the filter; `docs/how-it-works.md`'s two older sentences on a filter from the user's own config ("keeps running", "runs as usual") now point at the four names. The same file says `GIT_CONFIG` is dropped, and why. SECURITY.md, README.md and the rest of MANUAL.md name no variable (checked by grep) and are unchanged. CHANGELOG.md gets its lines at the release step.
- The tests now, besides the above. In real repos: with `GIT_CONFIG` naming an empty file and a file with `trailmark.asked`, a filter named `set` is `odd-filter-name`, an ordinary filter is switched off (1 counted) and a repo with no filter is read, no marker written; then `git status` itself is run in the ordinary repo and must write the marker (the forced fault). `gitEnv` asked directly: of the seven `GIT_CONFIG` names it gets, it keeps the five named above and drops `GIT_CONFIG` in capitals and in small letters. The helper that sets a variable for one call puts back what was there, also after a failure.
- Mutations after the batch, the whole list after the last change to the tests (CONFIRMED here, kept as `mutate-filtername-r2.txt`), none other than listed before the run:
  - `gitbin.js` (`mutate-filtername-env.js`), 5 probes. 4 fail the real-repos test in both copies, as listed (the decision test stands in for git and never reads the environment): `GIT_CONFIG` not dropped, which is the file before this fix; only `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` dropped; every variable that starts with `GIT_CONFIG` dropped; `GIT_CONFIG` dropped in capitals only. The unchanged file passes.

- The clones: each had an empty `git status --short` and an empty `tmp-review` (checked here before they were removed).

The assertion now hands `gitEnv` a count of 1 with `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_0` and `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` and holds all three.
- Fixed, Opus 3 (INFERRED by Opus, minor): under `GIT_CONFIG`, the repos with a filter named `set` said "did not run" and were not followed by git itself. Now each of them is, like the ordinary ones (PLAYBOOK "Testing" step 8, per case).
- Docs (Sonnet): nothing wrong; four points under "could be clearer", read against the code here and taken. MANUAL.md's row names the system's config beside the repo's and the user's, and shows a line for the attributes file (`*.bin filter=newname`). `docs/how-it-works.md` says `git status` is skipped where it said a filter under one of the four names does not keep running, in both sentences, and says "no name" where it said "nothing".
- DEFERRED, RS-A-redirect, SERIOUS (a repo's filter command runs), older than this branch, flagged to the user. CONFIRMED by Fable on 69d4eb4 and 11391c1; CONFIRMED here on 69d4eb4 (`probe-redirect.js`, git 2.56.1.windows.1, 17:03, each row with a control where plain git runs the filter):
  - `GIT_REDIRECT_STDIN=<an empty file>` in the session's environment (a variable of Git for Windows): `git check-attr --stdin` reads that file in place of the paths it is sent and answers nothing, `filterOverrides` takes that for "no file has a filter", and `git status` runs the repo's filter. Seen: status read, 1 counted, no filter switched off, marker written, for a filter named `evil` and for one named `set`.
  - `GIT_REDIRECT_STDERR=2>&1` with an index extension git skips (`ABCD` and four zero bytes appended to `.git/index`, checksum redone; the filter named in `.git/info/attributes`): the warning lands in front of the first path `git ls-files` prints, check-attr is asked about a file that is not there, and the filter runs. Seen: 2 counted, marker written, for `evil` and for `set`.
  - `GIT_REDIRECT_STDOUT=<a file>`: `outside-worktree`, nothing runs (status is read in no repo).
  - A repo's files cannot set these variables: they need the user's environment (INFERRED, as for `GIT_CONFIG`).
  - Not in this PR: it is older, 69d4eb4 answers as 11391c1 does under these variables (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run on 11391c1), and nothing this PR adds rests on it. By the delegation of 2026-11-11 a CONFIRMED serious finding is the user's to decide. Asked "Fix the two older redirect-variable gaps next, as their own PR?", the user answered "Yes, fix next" (2026-11-12): it is the next item after this PR merges, on a branch of its own.
  - The fix to make (INFERRED, Fable and here): drop the three names in `gitEnv`, and have `filterOverrides` take the answer of check-attr only when it has one answer for each path sent, in order. The second part does not cover the stderr case (the paths sent are already wrong there), so both parts are needed.
- The class, searched this time in the program and not in its pages (CONFIRMED here, `scan-wide-names.js` and `grep -a` on the installed `git.exe` 2.56.1): 194 names that start with `GIT_` are stored as plain text, and exactly three as 16-bit text, the three `GIT_REDIRECT_` names, which is why a search for plain text does not find them. Of the plain ones, the checks and `git status` agreed or failed closed under every one tried here and by Fable: the `GIT_CONFIG_*` family, `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE`, `GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM`, the pathspec variables, `GIT_INDEX_VERSION`, `GIT_IMPLICIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`. The trace variables leave what `git ls-files -z` prints unchanged and write to stderr (CONFIRMED here with raw git, 17:09, eleven settings: `GIT_TRACE` as `1`, `true` and `2`, `GIT_TRACE2`, `GIT_TRACE2_EVENT`, `GIT_TRACE2_PERF`, and five of the `GIT_TRACE_*` names). Not gone through one by one: the `GIT_TEST_*` names.
- DEFERRED, RS-T-repoatconfig (Opus 2, INFERRED, older: `repoAt` in `plugins/*/tests/handoff-checks.test.js` is the same on 11391c1): on a machine that has `GIT_CONFIG` set, the helper's `git config user.email` and its like write into the user's file and not into the test repo. Mechanism CONFIRMED by Opus with raw git (`GIT_CONFIG=<file> git config user.email t@t` wrote to the file), not re-run. Repro: that command in a scratch repo. Fix to weigh: the tests' own git calls drop the variable, or `dev/release-check.sh` unsets it.
- Held, by Fable (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run): `GIT_CONFIG` as an empty file, with `trailmark.asked`, defining filters, missing, a folder, a relative path, empty, and in other capitals; a repo's own `trailmark.asked` in 15 shapes; every caller of `gitEnv` and every `git config` call in `scripts/lib`; the `.lfsconfig` check, which gave `filter-check` under `GIT_CONFIG` on 11391c1 and answers now. Nothing else under `plugins/trailmark/scripts` starts git.
- Held, by Opus (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): with no `GIT_CONFIG`, both commits answer the same for an empty repo, no filter, an ordinary filter, LFS left running, `-filter`, a filter named `set`, a linked worktree of each, a superproject and a submodule with its own filter; with it, 69d4eb4 gives its answers without the variable. 11391c1's `gitbin.js` fails the real-repos test for the fault. ## Next version: RS-A-filterkey (2026-11-12, branch `claude/rs-a-filterkey` off 815abc4)

PR #53 (RS-A-deepindex) merged as 5a15974 at 2026-11-12 14:54:46Z, through auto-merge, with 9 of 9 jobs passed on its last head 815abc4 (CONFIRMED here from `gh pr view`). It merged under the user's delegation of 2026-11-11: round 3 (Opus, on ef2fb0a..815abc4, the test and the notes only) found nothing wrong (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run: the test passed 3 of 3 in each copy; every change of the table that the test's comment lists failed it; each member the comment names has a pair of names in the case; the added lines hold no raw non-ASCII character; no sentence of the notes was untrue). The merge command was a step of its own, after the pushed head showed its 9 jobs. Round 3's note, not a finding: names keyed after `trimEnd()` pass the test. That is outside the test's stated list ("a space before it"). On Windows a tracked name with a space at its end is `odd-path` before it reaches the tree (`dotEnd` in `gitbin.js` is for Windows only, read here), so one list of names cannot hold that member on every system; PLAYBOOK "Writing code and docs" step 11 says so now. The round's clone had an empty `git status --short` and an empty `tmp-review` (checked here before it was removed).

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 766ef8a (`probe-filterkey.js`: the library in a process of its own, then the real PreCompact hook, each with a control without the setting) and again on 815abc4 by the new test: with a tracked file that has `filter=lfs` and a repo config `filter.lfs.constructor=x`, `gitFacts(dir, ['status'])` threw `TypeError: LFS_STANDARD[e.key].test is not a function` and the PreCompact hook exited 1 with nothing printed. No code of the repo's ran and nothing more was read. That compaction got no snapshot.
- Why: `LFS_STANDARD` in `gitfacts.js` was a plain object from a setting's last word to a pattern for its value, and `filterOverrides` asked `LFS_STANDARD[e.key] && LFS_STANDARD[e.key].test(e.value)`. Every plain object answers `constructor` with the function `Object`, which is something, and has no `test`.
- The class (PLAYBOOK "Writing code and docs" step 11): a plain object looked up by a word that comes from outside the code. By reading `scripts/lib`, the hooks and the launcher:
  - From a repo: this one lookup. Of the names every object has, only `constructor` can be a setting's last word. git takes letters, digits and `-` there with a letter first, refuses `__proto__`, and prints the word in lower case, so `toString` arrives as `tostring` (CONFIRMED here, `probe-filterkey-keys.sh`).
  - Not from a repo (seen in the code, nothing run): `HOOKS[name]` in `trailmark.js` and `HINTS[kind]` in `hook-milestone.js` take a word from the command line, which the plugin's own hooks file writes; `NODE_FLOORS[v[0]]` takes node's own version; `COMMIT_NOTES[e.note]` takes a word that was asked with `hasOwnProperty` first; `state.sessions[sid]` is asked only after `cleanSession` held the id to a UUID's pattern; `old[d]` and `swept[x]` in `store.js` are used only when the answer is an array or a finite number; front matter is read by fixed names; `hookio.pick` and `PS_LOC_PARAMS` ask with `hasOwnProperty`.
  - In the same function, the two tables keyed by a filter's name (`names`, `repoKeys`) were Maps already. No test held that. The new test does (filters named `constructor` and `__proto__`), and the mutation list swaps each for a plain object.
- Fix, `gitfacts.js`, both copies: `LFS_STANDARD` is a Map, asked with `has` and `get`. A setting whose last word is not in the table is one the repo added, whatever the word: the lfs filter is switched off for the status call and counted in `statusFiltersOff`, as it already was for a word such as `foo`.
- After, CONFIRMED here (the same probe on the fixed tree): `gitFacts` answers with status read, and the PreCompact hook exits 0, with the setting and without it.
- Test, `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies: "git status: a filter setting git does not know is never taken as a standard LFS one, whatever its name (RS-A-filterkey)". Three small real repos, one for each filter name (`lfs`, `constructor`, `__proto__`), each with one tracked file that uses the filter and with the filter's `process` and `required` set to standard values; a case adds its setting and takes it out again. It reads `statusSkipped` and `statusFiltersOff`. Left running: the lfs filter with two and with three standard settings, and once more after the other cases. Switched off: `filter.lfs.smudge=cat`, `filter.lfs.foo=x`, `filter.lfs.constructor=x`, `filter.lfs.constructor` with a standard value, and the filters named `constructor` and `__proto__`. Its comment says what it holds apart and that it tells nothing of what git-lfs does when it runs.
- On 815abc4's `gitfacts.js` the test fails 2 of 2 with the TypeError above (CONFIRMED here: the first probe of the mutation list, its output read). That run is the reproduction.
- The test does not need git-lfs. Its first draft set `required` to `true` in one case and left it out in another, and `git status` stops (exit 128, `clean filter 'lfs' failed`) when a required filter cannot start; with `required` false it answers (exit 0) (CONFIRMED here, `probe-filterkey-nolfs.sh`, a process that does not exist; left out, it stopped too, since Git for Windows has `filter.lfs.required=true` in its system config, read here). Found by reading before the first push: the sweep suite's test of LFS left running skips without git-lfs (`HAVE_LFS`) and on Windows. Every repo of the test now sets `required` to `false`, a standard value. On Linux without git-lfs (WSL Ubuntu, Node 18.19.1, git 2.56.1, a copy of `plugins/trailmark`, `wsl-filterkey-test.sh`) the test passes 3 of 3 (CONFIRMED here).
- Mutations (`mutate-filterkey.js`, each through `dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-filterkey'`, 12 probes, three whole runs with the same result, the last after the test's last change, CONFIRMED here): 10 caught 2 of 2, one passes as meant, and the unchanged fix passes. Caught: `gitfacts.js` of 815abc4; the table as a plain object with the old lookup; a plain object asked with `in`; a plain object where what is no pattern counts as standard; a word the table does not have counted as standard; a word the table has counted as standard with any value; `some` for `every`; a filter under any name left running when its settings are standard; `repoKeys` as a plain object; `names` as a plain object. Passes as meant: a plain object asked with `hasOwnProperty`, which is the same fix written another way.
- Noted, the same on 815abc4 and not changed: a setting whose last word has a digit or a `-` (`filter.lfs.x1`, `filter.lfs.x-y`) does not match the pattern the settings are read with and is not counted as the repo's, so the lfs filter stays on with it, where `foo` switches it off. No filter runs that would not run anyway (INFERRED: git's own page on attributes names `clean`, `smudge`, `process` and `required` for a filter and no other word, read here in the installed copy).
- No shipped doc changes. `docs/how-it-works.md` says Git LFS keeps running when the repo's own config has "exactly what `git lfs install --local` writes", which holds; MANUAL.md and SECURITY.md say nothing of single settings (checked by grep).
- Not shown: a run on macOS. CI runs the test there.
- `--sums --quick` passed on this tree (exit 0, observed 10:13). Its handoff-checks step took 682 s, against 514 to 536 s in the four runs of the item before. Unexplained: the new test takes under 19 s for both copies with the start of node counted (`--only`), and WSL was up during the earlier runs too. No other check ran at the same time. To settle: the time of the next `--sums --quick`.

### RS-A-filterkey round 1 (2026-11-12, on 815abc4..b91fc18, PR #54)

Fable on the security of `gitfacts.js` and Opus on regressions, each from a clone of its own; no docs row. Nothing CONFIRMED wrong in the diff. The round brought out two older faults, one of them serious, and an older gap in the tests. This batch closes the gap: it changes the test, these notes, `LESSONS.md` and `PLAYBOOK.md` only, and `gitfacts.js` stays as b91fc18 has it. CI on b91fc18: 9 of 9 jobs passed (seen in `gh pr view`).

- FLAGGED, serious, RS-A-filtername, not fixed here and for the user to decide (told on 2026-11-12): a filter the repo defines runs when its name is `set`, `unset`, `unspecified` or empty. `filterOverrides` takes those values of `git check-attr -z` for "no filter" (`gitfacts.js`, the line `if (!v || v === 'unspecified' || v === 'unset' || v === 'set') continue;`), and git prints the same text for a filter so named (`*.bin filter=set`, `*.bin filter=`). With `[filter "set"]` (or `[filter ""]`) and a `clean` command in the repo's own `.git/config`, the status call gets no `-c filter.<name>.clean=`, `statusFiltersOff` is not set, and the command runs. CONFIRMED by Fable on 815abc4 and b91fc18, through `trailmark.js --hook precompact` too. CONFIRMED here on b91fc18 and on the installed copy of 0.6.2 (`probe-filtername.js <temp base> <plugin folder>`, with `echo ran >> <marker>; cat` as the command): the marker is written for each of the four names and not for the control `evil`, which is counted as 1 off. The same line is in v0.5.0 and v0.6.2 (`git show <tag>:plugins/trailmark/scripts/lib/gitfacts.js`). What it needs: a folder that came with its own `.git/config`, such as an unpacked archive or a copied folder; a clone does not carry one. Fix idea (INFERRED, not tried): take those four values for names too when the repo's own config defines a filter so named, and note that the pattern the settings are read with (`filter\.(.+)\.`) does not match an empty name.
- Deferred, RS-A-hookword, low (CONFIRMED by Fable on b91fc18, not re-run; INFERRED by Fable the same on 815abc4, the file being unchanged): `trailmark.js --hook constructor` (or `toString`, `__proto__`) exits 1 with a TypeError, since `HOOKS[name]` is a plain object. The word comes from the plugin's own hooks file, never from a repo. The class check in the section "Next version: RS-A-filterkey" had it as not reachable from a repo, which holds; it said nothing of what the lookup does with such a word. Repro: the command itself.
- Fixed in this batch, an older gap (the same on 815abc4):
  - Opus 1, CONFIRMED by Opus and here (`mutate-filterkey.js . 'value: '` on b91fc18, kept as `mutate-filterkey-values-before.txt`): the test passed with a standard value's pattern loosened. Opus tried four (`clean` and `required` taking any value, `smudge` and `process` taking what starts with `git-lfs`); of 16 tried here, 15 passed. A loosened `clean` would leave a command the repo wrote running. On Windows no other test holds the patterns: the sweep suite's two tests of filters are for POSIX only.
  - The class, written down before the cases: for each word of the table, every standard form of its value, and a value that only starts or only ends with a standard one. The test has 14 new cases. Left running: `smudge` as installed and with `--skip`, `process` with `--skip`, `required` empty and in capitals, and `required` true (read by its count only, since git stops there without git-lfs). Switched off: `clean`, `smudge` and `process` each with more after the standard value and with more before it, and `required` set to `yes`. The helper puts a set-up setting back after a case that changed it, and a case after these reads the repo as installed again (the two cases of other filter names follow it). The comment says what is not told: a setting with no value at all, which only a config file written by hand has.
  - Mutations (one whole run after the change, 28 probes, CONFIRMED here): 26 caught 2 of 2, the same one passes as meant, and the unchanged fix passes. New and caught, 16: each of Opus's four; `clean`, `smudge` and `process` each not held at its end and not held at its start; `smudge` and `process` without `--skip`; `required` without capitals, without the empty value, without `false` and without `true`.
  - On Linux without git-lfs (WSL, as in the section above) the changed test passes 3 of 3 (CONFIRMED here). On Windows it takes 24 s for both copies through `--only`, against 19 s before.
  - `--sums --quick` passed on this batch's tree (exit 0, observed 11:03). Its handoff-checks step took 951 s, after 682 s for the first commit and 514 to 536 s for the item before. Not explained by this test, which adds 5 s; no other check ran at the same time. Unexplained, and being looked at before round 2 starts.
- Opus 2, low, not changed (INFERRED by Opus): with a git older than 2.26, which has no `--show-scope`, every filter is switched off, and the cases that expect the lfs filter left running would fail. The older tests of the same kind would too.
- Fable, sound on b91fc18 (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run): 181 repos compared between 815abc4's file, the new one and the codex copy, on the whole answer and on the status call's `-c` settings: 23 differ, each as "the old one throws, the new one switches the filter off", and none where the new one leaves running what the old one switched off; of the 12 names every object has, only `constructor` reaches the lookup; no throw in 25 shapes of config and attributes and 13 of `.lfsconfig`; through the real hook the new code exits 0 and the snapshot says a file uses a filter the repo's config defines, where 815abc4 exits 1. INFERRED by Fable, read and not run: the other lookups by a word from outside are guarded as the class check says.
- Opus, sound on b91fc18 (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): the test passes 3 of 3 in each copy (10.7 to 13.1 s) and fails on 815abc4's file with the TypeError; the eight listed changes fail it; git refuses `__proto__`, `a_b` and `1abc` as a key's last word, also written by hand, and prints `ToString` as `tostring`; a filter that cannot start stops git only when required; git-lfs left `lfs.repositoryformatversion` and `.git/lfs` in the test's first repo, which nothing in `filterOverrides` reads; no sentence of the notes was untrue.
- Both clones had an empty `git status --short` and an empty `tmp-review` (checked here before they were removed). `E:/DevTemp` held `jbmem-filterkey-Uwji9Y` (10:26, the three repos of this test), which no check of a clone shows; removed here. INFERRED: a reviewer's run with kept folders in a shell without the three variables. The lesson "A reviewer's test run left a folder in the machine's temp folder" came back, so it is a PLAYBOOK step now ("Reviewing" step 2): the brief gives the test command as one line with the three variables in front of it.

## Next version: RS-A-deepindex (2026-11-12, branch `claude/rs-a-deepindex` off 4299c47, merged in PR #53 as 5a15974)

PR #52 (RS-A-worktreeslinks) merged as 4299c47 at 2026-11-12 07:23:56Z, through auto-merge, with 9 of 9 jobs passed on its last head 8110e55 (CONFIRMED here from `gh pr view`). It merged under the user's delegation of 2026-11-11 (a deferred item's PR merges once its review round is clean and the checks pass): round 6 (Sonnet, docs, on dac303c..8110e55, a test comment and these notes only) found nothing wrong (CONFIRMED by Sonnet, not re-run). The merge command was a step of its own, after the pushed head showed its 9 jobs. Round 6's two points that could be clearer, not changed: the test comment's "more than 4% of what an entry counts" stands next to "a cut-off below about 15,400 characters" without saying the 4% is of an entry's count of about 19,240; and the notes could say once that 96% is the worktrees check's own share and 97% the whole check's.

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 4299c47 (Windows, Node 26.13.1, git 2.56.1, `measure-deepindex.js`, three runs each, a library timing): `farWorktree` in `gitfacts.js` built `parts.slice(0, i).join('/')` for every level of every tracked path and looked it up in a Set, and nothing in that loop looked at the time. The work grows with the files times the square of the depth. Only an index is needed (`git update-index --index-info`, nothing checked out): with 1,500 files in one folder 1,500 deep, `gitFacts(r, ['status'], { budgetMs: 5000 })` took 12.18 to 12.24 s and answered `git-timeout`; 500 files 1,000 deep took 2.44 to 2.46 s; 500 files 1,000 deep, each in a last folder of its own, 2.54 s.
- Through the real PreCompact hook (`time-deepindex-hook.js`: the launcher started as a process with a home folder of its own, in a repo set up with `trailmark.js init --force`, parent and fix in turn, CONFIRMED here): 1,500 files 1,500 deep took 14.45 and 14.93 s on 4299c47, past the 10 s at which Claude Code stops a hook, and 1.53 and 1.47 s with the fix; 500 files 1,000 deep 3.01 and 2.88 s against 1.06 and 1.10 s; a repo with nothing tracked 0.86 to 0.92 s on both.
- On Linux (WSL Ubuntu, Node 18.19.1, git 2.56.1, the same library script, parent, fix, fix, parent, three runs each, CONFIRMED here): 1,500 files 1,500 deep took 26.86 to 28.55 s on 4299c47 (`git-timeout`) and 0.38 to 0.40 s with the fix, 1,500 counted. macOS is not timed; CI runs the test there.
- Fix, `gitfacts.js`, both copies:
  - The folders are kept as a tree of names (`tree = { path, kids: Map }`). Each level of a path costs one `kids.get` of that level's name, and a path string is built only when a folder is new. The list `folders` is an array filled in the same order as before (a folder after its parents), and `folders.length >= MAX_TREE_FOLDERS` answers `many-folders` as before. The work now follows the size of git's list, whatever its order.
  - The loop looks at the time once for each `LIST_STEP` (1,048,576) characters of git's list and answers `git-timeout` with under 800 ms left, the same floor as the loops after it. A list can be 32 MB (`maxBuffer`), so that is at most 32 looks; a list more than one character under 1 MB gets none (corrected in round 1: the empty piece after the last NUL counts 1).
  - `farWorktree` is exported for its test.
- With the fix (same script, three runs each, CONFIRMED here): the three shapes above take 1.08 to 1.11 s, 0.76 to 0.80 s and 0.84 to 0.98 s, with the right count each time. Larger: 100,000 files 20 deep 1.23 to 1.24 s; 19,900 files 100 deep, each in a folder of its own, 2.19 to 2.25 s; 5,000 files 3,000 deep (a list of 30 MB) 2.50 to 2.60 s, answer `git-error`.
- That `git-error` is `git status` itself, CONFIRMED here (`probe-deepindex-giterror.js`): with tracked paths of 4,122 characters or more, Git for Windows 2.56.1 stops with `BUG: compat/win32/fscache.c:133: Trying to allocate fsentry for long path` (exit 3221226505), and with 4,082 it answers. `rev-parse` and both `ls-files` calls answer at 6,002 characters. Status is left out with a word, as for any other git error. Not looked at: what git on Linux or macOS does with such paths.
- One very long path (`probe-deepindex-shapes.js`, a made-up `r`, CONFIRMED here): one tracked path of 16 MB with 19,000 folders ran node out of memory on 4299c47 (the old loop joins 19,000 paths of 8 MB on average). With the fix the list is gone through, and the loop over the folders, which looks at the time before each one, answers `git-timeout` after 4.20 to 4.22 s of the 5 s given, with 76 to 182 MB in use. Names of 20 and of 100 characters at that depth give the same answer after 4.20 s. The fix adds a folder's name to its parent's path, and node keeps such a text as its two parts until it is used, so no whole path is built in the first loop; the test holds that by the memory in use. One path of 16 MB with 8,000,000 folders answers `many-folders` in 0.31 to 0.32 s (2.13 and 2.55 s on 4299c47).
- The class, by reading every step between `ls-files` and `git status` (lesson "A fix covered the reviewer's example, not its class"): the first loop's other work for an entry follows the entry's length, and now has the look at the time; the loop that fills `byFolder` does two Map lookups (`has` and `get`, corrected in round 1) of the folder's whole path for each entry and has no look; the `byFolder` loop looks every 500 folders; the `folders` loop looks before each folder; `filterOverrides` follows the list and runs git under timeouts. No other step was found whose work grows faster than the list. The loop without a look is covered by the 30 MB run above for a list of many entries: the whole call took 2.50 to 2.60 s, git included. (Corrected in round 1: it is not covered for one name of 30 MB, RS-A-longname below.)
- Test, `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies: "git status: finding the folders of tracked files takes work that follows the list, and looks at the time (RS-A-deepindex)". It hands `farWorktree` a made-up `r` (`run` answers `rev-parse` and `ls-files` from a list built in the test, `left` is scripted and counted) and counts work, not seconds: the characters of every string key looked up in a Map or Set during the call. Cases: the folders are looked at in the order `a`, `a/b`, `a/b/c`, `a/x`, `z`; 200 files 400 deep look up less than 8 times the list's length and more than once (the fix: 399,600 characters for a list of 170,890, 2.34 times; the old loop: 32,319,600, 189 times); a list of 2.6 MB in one folder asks for the time 3 times and passes; with the second ask at 500 ms the answer is `git-timeout` after 2 asks and no folder is looked at; with it at 800 the answer is `''`; a short list asks once; 20,001 folders answer `many-folders` with no folder looked at; one path 1,500 folders deep with names of 400 characters (a list of 600,000 characters, whose folders' paths add up to 450 MB) grows the memory in use by less than 100 times the list before the first look at the time.
- The test fails on 4299c47 only because `farWorktree` is not exported there (a TypeError), which proves nothing about the fix. The proof is the mutation that puts the old loop back in the new file: it fails 2 of 2.
- Mutations (`mutate-deepindex.js`, each through `dev/release-check.sh --only 'RS-A-deepindex'`, 14 probes, one whole run after the last case was added, CONFIRMED here): 13 caught 2 of 2 and the unchanged fix passes. Caught: the old loop back; no look at the time; a look for every entry; a look for each 1,048,576 entries in place of characters; `LIST_STEP` 100 times larger; `LIST_STEP` half as large; the floor at 250 ms; the floor at 900 ms; a new folder's path without its parent; a new folder's whole path joined from its parts (`parts.slice(0, i + 1).join('/')`, which only the memory case tells); no limit on the folders; the file's own name taken for a folder; `gitfacts.js` of 4299c47.
- No shipped doc changes. MANUAL.md, SECURITY.md and docs/how-it-works.md name `many-folders` and `git-timeout` and say nothing about this loop or its time (checked by grep), and the answers are unchanged. CHANGELOG.md gets its lines at the release step, as for the earlier deferred items.
- Not shown: a time on macOS. Not settled, and not worth its cost: whether git accepts an index whose entries are out of order (INFERRED: it checks the order only in `fsck`). Making one means writing the index file by hand, and the answer changes nothing: the tree's work does not depend on the order, and the test's first case hands the entries over out of order.
- Lesson logged: "A hook timing read the same on the old code and the fix, because the hook had left early".
