## Next version: RS-A-worktreeslinks (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-worktreeslinks` off 9e78e0a, merged in PR #52 as 4299c47)

PR #51 (RS-A-firstcheck) merged as 195739f at 2026-11-12 02:45:08Z, through auto-merge, with 9 of 9 jobs passed on its last head 9e78e0a (CONFIRMED here from `gh pr view`). It merged without the user being asked by name: on 2026-11-11 the user delegated that ("I would like to delegate that decision to you", scope confirmed with "Yes, that scope is right, continue": a deferred item's PR merges once its review round is clean and the checks pass). Its round 3 (Sonnet, docs, on d231cf3..9e78e0a) found nothing wrong in the two phrases round 2 changed (CONFIRMED by Sonnet, not re-run). The merge command was a step of its own, after the pushed head showed its 9 jobs.

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 9e78e0a (Windows): `otherWorktrees` in `gitbin.js` ran under no limit on the work, only a count of 10,000 names. It reads two pointer files for each entry of a `worktrees` folder and walks each path from up to three folders, and none of that work was counted. (Corrected in round 1: the looks at the disk were already kept for the whole check, through `withShare`; the time was the work on each path's text.) `repro-worktreeslinks.js` (a repo, N folders below it, one inside the other, each with a `.git` git doesn't accept whose `worktrees` is a junction to one folder of 400 entries, each with a `gitdir` file naming a 900-character path; `farGitConfig` from the innermost folder): 1 such folder 3.71 s, 4 folders 13.83 s, answer `''` both times. Through the real PreCompact hook (`time-wt-hook.js`, the launcher started as a process with a home folder of its own): 1 folder 4.12 and 3.59 s, 4 folders 7.69 and 7.31 s, none 0.17 to 0.18 s. For 4 folders the hook's time is lower than the library's. INFERRED from the code and the arithmetic, not traced: since RS-A-firstcheck the hook's first check looks at its 5 s before each config file is read, each folder's walk takes about 3.5 s, so the look before the third folder's config finds the time gone, at about 7 s. The library call has no clock.
- Fix, `gitbin.js`, both copies:
  - `farGit` makes the repo's limit (`work = { left: MAX_PATH_WORK }`, or the one handed in) before the worktrees check, and runs the check as `limitedWalk(() => otherWorktrees(...), work)`. The check, the config values and the alternates then share one limit for the repo, and on the way up from the session's folder every `.git` found shares it too (RS-A-abovegit's `above`). What is new is that the walks count: what the disk showed was already kept (corrected in round 1).
  - `otherWorktrees` counts `WORKTREE_COST` (12 looks at the disk) for each name in the folder before anything is read for it, and answers `many-worktrees` once the limit is used up. A `many-config-paths` from the walks inside it is answered as `many-worktrees` too, so the word names what was being checked.
  - `MAX_WORKTREES` (10,000 names) stays as a first refusal before anything is read. The limit on the work is reached far sooner (about 2,700 names with no pointers).
- Every caller of `farGit`, CONFIRMED here by reading (`grep 'farGit('`): the way up in `farGitConfigIn` and `farBareProbe` hand in the shared limit; `farNestedIn` hands in none, so each nested repo or submodule folder has a limit of its own, as its config values already had; the two calls for submodule gitdirs are `light` and the call from `localRepoRoot` is `locate`, and neither reaches the worktrees check.
- A decision, mine under the delegation: the cost of an entry. A first draft counted 3 looks at the disk. 100 worktrees made by `git worktree add` then counted 156,236 and took 0.08 to 0.33 s, so the limit would have been reached only after 4 to 17 s of such entries. At 12 looks the limit is reached at about 1 to 2 s of them, in line with what the limit allows for config values.
- A change in behavior, and its label: a repo with more than about 2,000 worktrees now has git kept out (`many-worktrees`), where up to 10,000 were let through. Counted here with a probe copy of the library (`measure-worktrees.js`): 50 worktrees made by `git worktree add` count 193,436 under `E:/DevTemp/wtlinks-work` and 215,596 under a folder 149 characters long, so 1,945 to 2,168 fit (INFERRED from 50); 100 count 386,636 (4.6%); this repo's 3 count 12,329. That no real repo has that many is INFERRED: two web searches on 2026-11-11 found no report of a repo with hundreds or thousands of worktrees, which is no proof there is none. One worktree for each agent run is the likeliest way to get there, and `git worktree prune` removes the entries of worktrees whose folders are gone (MANUAL.md says so now). A repo with many worktrees and many path settings reaches the limit sooner, since they share it.
- After, CONFIRMED here (Windows):
  - The library: the shape above is refused in 1.16 to 1.39 s for 1 and for 4 such folders.
  - The real hook, old and new in turn (old, new, new, old; the old code from `git archive 9e78e0a`): 1 folder 1.49 and 1.49 s, 4 folders 1.40 and 1.38 s, none 0.17 to 0.18 s.
  - The slowest shape the limit lets through (`search-wt-slowest.js`, one folder below the repo with a junction to a worktrees folder that grows by 300, two checks at each size from a fresh tree; four runs, not every shape in each): entries that are empty folders, 2,700 let through in 0.27 to 0.29 s (one run); entries with pointers that say `.`, 1,800 in 0.57 to 0.79 s (three runs; 0.70 and 1.15 s in the first); entries as git writes them, 2,100 in 0.57 and 0.85 s (one run; 2,000 took 1.82 s the first time in an earlier run of `repro-worktreeslinks.js`); the same with the gitdir 12 folders deep, 2,100 in 0.57 and 0.66 s (one run); entries that are links to folders of their own, 1,500 in 0.80 to 1.39 s (two runs). The next size up is refused for each.
  - One reading is unexplained: in the first of the four runs the `.` shape took 2.22 and 1.64 s at 1,200 entries and 3.64 and 1.32 s at 1,500, and under 0.8 s in the three runs after it, one of them in the first run's order (so the removal of 3,000 folders just before it is ruled out as the cause alone). HYPOTHESIZED: other work on the disk at that time. It is a timing of a probe, not a test.
- Linux, CONFIRMED here in WSL (ext4 under `/tmp`, Node 18.19.1, git 2.43.0; `wsl-wt-timing.sh`, the library only): the parent's code lets the fault's shape through after 2.52 s for 1 folder, 9.45 s for 4 and 19.26 s for 8; the fix refuses it in 0.97 and 0.91 s for 1 and 4. The search, three runs with the same answers: the slowest shape let through is 1,500 entries that are links, 0.20 to 0.21 s; entries as git writes them are let through at 1,800 (0.13 to 0.14 s) and refused at 2,100, a step lower than on Windows. So "about 2,000" holds within one step of 300 on both systems, and PLAYBOOK "Testing" step 11 (time the limit on Windows and Linux) is met for the library. The real hook was not timed on Linux.
- Test: "git pointers: the check of other worktrees counts against the repo's limit on the work (RS-A-worktreeslinks)", `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies. A worktrees folder of 25 entries whose pointers name a 900-character path, reached through a link, is about a third of the limit for each `.git`: the repo and one `.git` below it are let through, the repo and four are refused. 2,400 plain files in a repo's own `worktrees` are let through and 3,100 are refused. 40 entries shaped like git's own are let through. It takes about 9.4 s for each copy here (its neighbors: RS-A-altmodules 12.5 s, RS-A-abovegit 30 s).
- Mutations (`mutate-worktreeslinks.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-worktreeslinks'`, a probe caught only with a count of failed tests): `gitbin.js` of 9e78e0a fails 2 of 2. Caught, 2 of 2 each: the check under no limit; a limit of its own for each worktrees check; a limit of its own for each `.git`; out of work answered with the config values' word; an entry counting 0, 3, 10, 14 and 1,200 looks (so the cost is held between 11 and 13); only folders counted. Passes, as expected: the count going on past zero (`limitedWalk` gives the same answer, later) and no change. 13 probes, one whole run, nothing to run again. The comment above `otherWorktrees` was reworded after that run; the test was run again on the final text.
- Docs: MANUAL.md, the `many-worktrees` row. SECURITY.md, the bullet on each other worktree, and one sentence in the bullet on the limit on the work that points to it. The times in SECURITY.md are the real hook's.
- Not shown: macOS times; the real hook on Linux; real worktrees past 100 (the 2,000 is from the count of 50 and 100); a reviewer asked to beat the "1 to 2 seconds" (round 1's brief asks).
- Deferred, new, older behavior:
  - RS-H-watchrepeat (the context watch; HYPOTHESIZED, seen once in this session on 2026-11-11, not looked into): the watch said the session "has not written the handoff since the first request" at about 341k tokens, although the handoff had been written after its ask at about 329k. No repro yet: the first step is to read `contextwatch.js` for what counts as written since the ask (the session's record, or the handoff's time) against a write made through `--from`.

### RS-A-worktreeslinks round 1 (2026-11-11, on 9e78e0a..63accaa, PR #52)

Fable on the security of `gitbin.js`, Opus on regressions and design, Sonnet on the two docs, each from a clone of its own under `E:/DevTemp` with the diff and a brief. The round is not clean: the security review beat the time the docs claimed, and the regression review found two changes to the fix that the test let through. Nothing that was refused on 9e78e0a is let through on 63accaa (24 shapes with the same answer on both, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run). The Codex bot's code review and security review of 63accaa ended with no findings (its status comment, read here).

- Fixed in this batch:
  - Fable F1, CONFIRMED by Fable and reproduced here (`search-wt-slowest.js`, shape `text`): a worktrees folder whose entries are links to one folder with two pointers of 4 KB, each made of `a/../` pairs and ending in eleven `..`, was let through at 2,080 entries in 3.05 to 3.08 s here (3.6 to 3.8 s by Fable; the same on 9e78e0a). `gitdirAt` works on the pointer's text before the walk (`exactPointer`, `netText`, `oddRelative`, the join), and the walk counts only what Windows leaves of the text once it is joined, which here is next to nothing. So "1 to 2 seconds" in SECURITY.md was wrong. Fix, in `otherWorktrees`: before a pointer is tried, it counts `TRY_COST` and half its length for each folder it is tried from (`far`). After, three runs here: 390 entries are let through in 0.58 to 0.61 s and 520 are refused. Half the length and not a quarter, as `farPathValue` counts a config value: at a quarter Fable's probe let 818 entries through in 1.5 to 2.2 s, and at half the text counts about as much for its time as the other shapes do (about 11 counted for each microsecond; an entry as git writes it 10 to 14). A real pointer of 60 to 200 characters adds 67 to 117 to an entry's 3,900 (corrected in round 2: this said about 100 to 200), so "about 2,000" stands.
  - Fable F3, CONFIRMED here by reading `withShare`: this section said the parent made every look at the disk again for each entry. It did not: `LSTAT_SEEN` is live for the whole check through `withShare`, on the parent too. The parent's time was the uncounted work on each path's text. The two sentences are corrected in place (round 1), and so is what the fix's first bullet said about it.
  - Opus F1, CONFIRMED by Opus and reproduced here (probe "the limit made after the worktrees check": 2/2 pass on 63accaa's test): no case went through `farNested`, the one caller that hands in no limit, so the test did not hold where `farGit` makes the limit. With the line back where the parent had it, a nested repo's worktrees check would get a fresh limit for each of its two folders. New case: a folder whose `.git` file names a gitdir with a `commondir`; the gitdir alone with a worktrees folder of 50 long-pointer entries (two thirds of the limit) is let through, and with the same on the common folder it is refused.
  - Opus, mutation B, reproduced here (probe "every answer of the check given as many-worktrees": 2/2 pass on 63accaa's test): the older tests that would catch it need symlink rights and skip on this machine. New case, no link needed: one entry's `gitdir` naming `//evil.invalid/share/x/wt/.git` answers `network-gitdir`.
  - A case for F1: 700 entries, each a link to one folder with the two 4 KB pointers, through a linked worktrees folder: `many-worktrees` on Windows. On Linux and macOS `..` is taken where it stands, after a folder `a` that isn't there, so the first entry answers `network-gitdir` (derived from `localPath`: a missing part followed by `..` is refused; not run here, CI runs it).
  - Docs. MANUAL.md: the `many-worktrees` row says locked worktrees stay after `git worktree prune` and names `git worktree remove` for one that is still there (Opus F2, Sonnet); the `many-config-paths` row says the other worktrees use the same limit (Sonnet; CONFIRMED here by reading `farGit`: the settings can run out what the worktrees left, and the word is then `many-config-paths`). SECURITY.md: "(followed under `many-entries`)" for the links the limit does not count, "only the cap of 10,000 names", "when the limit runs out", the pointer's text, and the timing sentence, which now says what was measured, what the review found and what is the slowest left.
- Mutations after the batch (`mutate-worktreeslinks.js`, one whole run, 19 probes, none to run again): the 13 of the first run as before, and six new, each caught 2 of 2: a pointer's text not counted; counted once and not for each folder; at an eighth and at a quarter of its length; the limit made after the worktrees check; every answer of the check given as `many-worktrees`.
- Fable F2, deferred as RS-A-linkhops (older: `localPath`'s count for following a link, the same for config values; CONFIRMED by Fable, the shape reproduced here): `readlinkSync` is not counted, and a hop counts 64 and the new path's text. Pointers naming a chain of 39 junctions: 211 entries let through in 1.86 s by Fable (49,798 `readlinkSync` calls); here (`search-wt-slowest.js`, shape `chain`, the chain at a path of about 50 characters) 300 entries in 1.50 to 1.55 s and 360 refused. Fable's "more on a shorter path" (INFERRED) did not show at this path; a shorter one was not tried. Not fixed here: counting a hop higher moves the bounds of the tests of linked stores and alternates, which is a change of its own. SECURITY.md names it as the slowest shape left. Repro: `node search-wt-slowest.js <gitbin.js> <temp base> 60 600 chain`.
- Deferred, older, the same on 9e78e0a:
  - RS-A-pointertext (INFERRED here, small): `gitdirAt`'s other callers (the `.git` file and `commondir` of a module gitdir, of a nested repo, of a folder probed as a bare repo) do the same text work uncounted. One or two pointers for each gitdir, so about 0.2 ms each: 2,000 module gitdirs about 0.4 s. Not measured. Repro idea: module gitdirs whose `commondir` is the 4 KB text of F1.
  - RS-A-submoduleword (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): a submodule refused with `many-worktrees` is reported as `submodule-pointer` (`gitfacts.js`, where every refusal of a submodule's folder gets that word).
- Noted, no change:
  - CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run: 20 worktrees made by git count 78,672 under a 49-character base and 86,522 under one of 199 (2,132 and 1,939 fit); a session in a linked worktree counts 77,904, nothing twice; 50 worktrees with 2,000 submodule gitdirs use 1.75 to 2.47 million. At the documented worst case for submodules (about 6 million) about 600 worktrees still fit (INFERRED by Opus).
  - The test's time and bounds, CONFIRMED by Opus on 63accaa, not re-run: 7.4 to 9.9 s, five runs in each copy; passes with a temp folder 30 and 100 characters longer (longer ones can't be set up on Windows); passes in random order with its neighbors. With this batch it takes about 10 s for each copy here.
  - 11 and 13 looks for an entry pass the test, 10 and 14 fail (Opus counted, the two outer ones run here): a step of one moves the cut by about 8%.
  - `MAX_WORKTREES` removed passes the test (Opus): it can no longer be reached first, and it stays as a first refusal before anything is read.
  - `many-worktrees` is kept in `farCache` (Fable: rightly, it does not depend on time). No code outside `gitbin.js` reads the word.
- RS-H-watchrepeat, a second sighting (2026-11-11): the context watch asked for a checkpoint again right after a handoff write that was saved over budget (exit 2). HYPOTHESIZED: a write saved over budget does not count as written. Still not looked into.
- Lesson logged: "A reviewer beat 'the slowest found' with work the search never tried".

### RS-A-worktreeslinks round 2 (2026-11-11, on 63accaa..27e43a4, PR #52)

Fable on the security of `gitbin.js`, Opus on regressions and design, Sonnet on the two docs, each from a clone of its own under `E:/DevTemp` with the diff and a brief. The round is not clean: the security review beat the docs' time again, this time through the length of the worktrees folder's path. Nothing that was refused on 63accaa is let through on 27e43a4 (39 shapes with the same answer on both, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run), and the round 1 test passes in WSL, its `network-gitdir` case included (Fable). The Codex bot's reviews of 27e43a4: its status comment still names 63accaa (no findings there); nothing from it on 27e43a4, and the PR has no review comments (read here).

- Fixed in this batch:
  - Fable F1, CONFIRMED by Fable and reproduced here (`measure-longbase.js`, shape `pairs`): `far` counted a pointer's text and not the folder it is tried from, and `path.resolve(folder, text)` works through both (Fable: 152 microseconds at a folder of 69 characters, 1,096 at 15,922, 2,002 at 29,951, for a 4 KB pointer). With plain folders `<base>/<N folders named 'd' x 200>/r/.git/worktrees/pK/`, each `pK` with a `commondir` and a `gitdir` of 4,095 characters (`a/../` pairs, then enough `..` to reach the root), 27e43a4 let through 1,143 entries at a path of 3,869 characters in 1.84 to 2.21 s here, and 900 at 16,130 characters in 4.26 to 4.76 s (Fable: 2.1 s, 4.6 to 5.4 s, and 840 entries at 30,222 characters in 8.5 to 9.4 s). Not a regression: 63accaa takes the same time. So "0.6 seconds" in SECURITY.md held only for a short path.
  - Fable F2, CONFIRMED by Fable and reproduced here (shapes `files`, `empty`, `dots`): an entry's flat 12 looks at the disk count the same at any path, and a look takes longer the longer the path is. Here, for each entry at a path of 50, 3,869 and 16,130 characters: a plain file 0.03, 0.20 and 0.67 ms; an empty folder 0.43 and 1.5 ms at the two long ones; a folder with two short pointers 0.78 and 2.4 ms; one with two 4 KB pointers 0.57, 1.61 and 4.73 ms. The time grows in a straight line with the path's length, by up to 0.27 microseconds a character for the last shape.
  - The fix, two lines in `otherWorktrees`: an entry counts `WORKTREE_COST + top.dir.length` (the worktrees folder's path, where it leads), and `far` counts `TRY_COST + ((t.length + b.length) >> 1)` for each folder `b` a pointer is tried from. The weights are from those times: one character of the path for an entry and half of one for each of its two pointers make 2 for each character, against 0.27 microseconds, which is about 130 ns for each count at any length (a 4 KB pointer at a short path: about 77). The entry's own name is not counted: at most 255 characters, and no test could hold a term that small. After, three runs each (`measure-longbase.js`), the first run apart: at 3,869 characters 550 entries are let through in 1.08 to 1.11 s (first 1.57) and 600 refused; at 16,130, 190 in 1.16 to 1.18 s (first 1.43) and 230 refused; at 30,200, 110 in 1.16 to 1.28 s (first 1.33). An earlier set of runs, with the entry's name still in the count (under 0.1% apart), read 1.05 to 1.24 s for the same sizes, and 210 entries at 16,130 answered `many-config-paths`: the worktrees check passes and the settings checked after it run out. From that set: at 16,130 characters 420 plain files take 0.32 to 0.33 s, 420 empty folders 0.70 to 0.74 s and 220 folders with short pointers 0.59 to 0.63 s. Through a link (the repo at a short path, its worktrees folder a junction to the long one, so a pointer is tried from two folders): 420 entries with 4 KB pointers at 3,855 characters in 0.86 to 0.91 s, 460 refused.
  - Real repos, measured here (`measure-worktrees.js`): 100 worktrees made by `git worktree add` count 405,826 with the repo at a path of 46 characters (4.8% of the limit, 4,058 each, so 2,067 fit), and 20 count 96,854 with it at 197 characters (4,843 each, so 1,732 fit). On 27e43a4 Opus counted 4,003 and 4,317 each. The docs say about 2,000, and about 1,700 at a path of 200 characters.
  - The test: the plain-file case's upper size goes from 3,100 to 2,800, since the folder's path now counts with each entry: with the temp folder at 24 to 235 characters 2,400 files are 89% to 96% of the limit and 2,800 are 104% to 112%, and the case still tells 10 and 14 looks from 12 at any of them (at 3,100 the 10-look probe would have passed with a temp folder about 100 characters longer). Four assertions at the end, on Windows only (macOS has no path that long): two folders at the end of a path of about 3,100 characters, each a repo's worktrees folder through a junction (a junction's target ends near 4,000 characters, which sets the depth). Plain files: 1,150 `''` and 1,600 `many-worktrees`. Folders with two pointers of 40 `..`: 700 `''` and 1,000 `many-worktrees`. Shares of the limit, from a probe that prints what is left (`probe-longbase-count.js`): 84.2%, over, 82.1%, over; with a temp folder 100 characters longer 85.6% and 84.6%. Git stops working in a temp folder much longer than that (`git init`: "Filename too long" at 235), so no longer one can run the test at all.
  - Opus F1, CONFIRMED by Opus and reproduced here (`search-wt-slowest.js`, shape `text`, three runs): 500 entries of round 1's shape are let through in 0.74 to 0.82 s on 27e43a4, so "0.6 seconds" (read at 390 entries) was too low. SECURITY.md says "up to about 0.8 seconds".
  - Docs (Sonnet; each checked here against the code). SECURITY.md: a pointer counts half its text's length (not "by its length"), and now half the folder's; "found since" for "left"; the 4 KB shape is described as `a/../` pairs that Windows reduces to almost nothing; the 0.8 names its system; a sentence on what round 2 found and what is let through at long paths. MANUAL.md: the `many-config-paths` row said a repo with very many worktrees "can get this word too", which read as if the worktrees check gave it; it gives `many-worktrees`, and what it used is gone for the settings checked after it (CONFIRMED here: 210 entries at 16,130 characters answer `many-config-paths`). `git worktree remove` needs `--force` for a worktree with changes or untracked files (CONFIRMED here in a scratch repo, git 2.56.1: "contains modified or untracked files, use --force to delete it").
  - Notes (Opus F2, CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): the round 1 section said a real pointer adds "about 100 to 200" to an entry; on 27e43a4 it added 67 to 117 (20 real worktrees counted 80,052 and 86,342). Corrected in place.
- Mutations after the batch (`mutate-worktreeslinks.js`, one whole run, 26 probes): 24 caught 2 of 2, and the two controls pass (the count going on past zero, no change). The 19 of round 1 as before, the four on a pointer's text rewritten for the new line. Seven new: the folder not counted with a pointer, at an eighth of its length, and at its whole length; the folder not counted with an entry, at half its length, and at twice its length; `gitbin.js` of 27e43a4 (round 1's).
- Noted, no change:
  - Opus F3 (CONFIRMED by Opus, not re-run): the test holds the weight of a pointer's text only between a quarter and a third (a third passes at 3% over), and lets through four other changes to `far` that count nearly the same (the charge made after the joins, `TRY_COST` dropped, three folders always, an absolute pointer charged once). The same holds for the folder's weight in `far` now: an eighth is caught and the whole length is caught; a quarter makes the 1,000-entry case 99.5% of the limit by my arithmetic, so the test does not tell a quarter from a half reliably.
  - The test's time: 10.3 to 12.9 s for each copy on 27e43a4 (Opus); with this batch 28 to 32 s for the two copies through `--only` (three runs).
  - Fable, sound on 27e43a4 (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run): once `far` takes the count below zero at most one `gitdirAt` and one `localPath` call follow, and the answer is `many-worktrees`; the trim before `far` costs 10.7 ms once on 4,094 line breaks; 509 link entries from three folders take 0.84 to 0.85 s; 2,587 entries with 4-character pointers 0.31 to 0.34 s.
- Unexplained slow readings (the second sighting of "A probe's slow reading came once in four runs"): in one series of timing runs here, 700 folders with short pointers through a link at 3,051 characters read 8.14, 4.65 and 5.75 s and then 0.97 to 1.25 s with 100 entries more; 380 entries with 4 KB pointers read 5.68, 2.20 and 1.22 s; and the count probe once read 4.61 s for a shape that read 0.92 to 2.05 s in three other runs. Two repeats of the same series, back to back, with the processor use of every process sampled each second (`cpu-sample.ps1`), read 0.43 to 0.56 s and 0.76 to 0.91 s (first runs 0.79 to 1.11 s), so the slow readings were not reproduced. Seen in the samples: Defender (`msmpeng`) at 118% to 236% of a core while the files are made and for some seconds after. HYPOTHESIZED: other work on the same drive at that time (other sessions use `E:/DevTemp`), or Defender behind on files just made. Not settled: it did not come back to be measured. The times in SECURITY.md are runs after the first, and it says so.
- Deferred, older, the same on main:
  - RS-A-longgitdir (CONFIRMED here on this branch's head; `linkedEntries` is not in this PR's diff, so the same on main, INFERRED): the walk of a gitdir for links stops at a number of entries (100,000, `many-entries`) whatever their path's length, and a look at the disk takes longer the longer the path is. With a repo made by `git init` moved to `<base>/<80 folders named 'd' x 200>/r` (a path of 16,122 characters) and empty files in `r/.git/x`, `farGitConfig(r)` answers `''` after 5.31 to 5.38 s for 30,000 files and 16.42 to 16.84 s for 99,000 (three runs each; 0.08 to 0.12 s with none; 3,000 empty folders there take 1.0 s). At a short path the same walk takes well under a second. Slower than anything this PR fixes, and not a data exposure or code that runs: a hook is held, on a tree someone has to have built at a path that long (git itself cannot work there). Fix idea: the walk's budget counts the path's length with each entry, as the worktrees check does now, or the walk looks at the hook's clock (RS-A-walktime). Repro: `node measure-longbase.js <gitbin.js> <temp base> 80 git-files 30000 99000` (the session's scratch folder), or the shape above by hand.
  - The same root in smaller places, not measured (INFERRED): `farBareProbe`, `usable` and `readSmall` on a gitdir's own files look at the disk a fixed number of times for each gitdir, so a long path adds milliseconds there, not seconds.

Two older folders of the same kind are there from other items (`jbmem-config-time-OzewAR`, 2026-11-11 16:59; `jbmem-far-fold-8dmTjO`, 2026-11-10) and are left for the user to say.
- Lessons logged: "The second 'slowest found' was beaten too, by the length of a path" (PLAYBOOK "Testing" step 13 extended; step 14 added from the slow readings), "A reviewer's test run left a folder in the machine's temp folder".

### RS-A-worktreeslinks round 3 (2026-11-12, on 27e43a4..2040118, PR #52)

Fable on the security of `gitbin.js`, Opus on regressions and design, Sonnet on the two docs, each from a clone of its own. No fault in the shipped code, and no refusal became `''` (34 shapes on both commits, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run; one differs as meant: 900 good entries at 3,000 characters before a share answer `many-worktrees` where 27e43a4 answered `network-gitdir`). The round is not clean: the test had two gaps, fixed in this batch (test and docs only, no change to `gitbin.js`), and the security review found two slower shapes, deferred.

- Fixed in this batch:
  - Opus F2, CONFIRMED by Opus and reproduced here (two probes passed 2/2 on 2040118's test): with the folder's length cut off at 4,096, in the entry's count or in `far`, the test passed. A junction's target stops near 4,071 characters (Opus), so the junction cases never saw a longer path, and the fix was drawn by 16,130. New: two repos made by hand (HEAD, config, `objects`, `refs`: git cannot work at such a path) at the end of a path of about 16,200 characters, with no link. Plain files in `.git/worktrees`: 330 `''`, 500 `many-worktrees`. Folders with two pointers of 110 `..`: 180 `''`, 270 `many-worktrees`. The junction case of plain files (1,150 and 1,600) is taken out; the junction case of folders (700 and 1,000) stays, since it is the one where a pointer is tried from two folders.
  - Opus F1, CONFIRMED by Opus (the folder at a quarter of its length in `far` is caught with a temp folder of 46 characters and let through at 76 and 146); here it was caught at the release check's temp folder, the longer ones not re-run. The 270-entry case holds it at any temp folder: 90% of the limit at a quarter by my arithmetic, and the probe is caught.
  - Mutations (`mutate-worktreeslinks.js`, one whole run after the change, 29 probes): 27 caught 2 of 2, the two controls pass. New: the folder at a quarter of its length with a pointer, and the folder cut off at 4,096 with a pointer and with an entry. Each failed run kept folders at 16,200 characters, and the release check's `rm -rf` cleared them (27 failing runs in a row; bash also removed a tree 6,060 characters deep in a try of its own).
  - Docs (Sonnet, INFERRED unclear, each checked against the two lines): SECURITY.md says an entry counts 12 looks "plus one for each character of the folder's path" and a pointer "half the sum of the lengths of its text and that folder's path" for each folder; "(measured)" on the two times; "Another slow shape found" for "The slowest found since", which followed a larger number. The MANUAL.md rows were found accurate.

- Deferred, with the new sentence in SECURITY.md saying what was measured:
  - RS-A-widepath (Fable F1; CONFIRMED by Fable and reproduced here on 2040118; in this branch's count, not a regression: 27e43a4 counts the same shape at 9% of the limit): the count goes by characters, and a path with a character outside Latin-1 takes about 1.7 times as long for each (`path.resolve(folder, pointer)` 4.0 to 4.2 ms against 2.1 to 2.4, Fable). Here (`LB_CODE=4e2d node measure-longbase.js <gitbin.js> <temp base> 150 pairs 105`, folders named with U+4E2D): 105 entries at 30,200 characters in 1.97 to 2.04 s against 1.08 to 1.15 s with the letter d; 540 at 3,869 in 1.64 to 1.66 s (first 2.00). Fable's slowest: 106 entries with 255-character names at 32,429, `''` after 2.38 to 2.47 s; re-run here (`LB_CODE=4e2d LB_NAME=255 ... 161 pairs 106`, the folder at 32,411): 2.21 to 2.28 s.
  - RS-A-edgepath (found here on 2040118 while re-running that; CONFIRMED as a time, cause HYPOTHESIZED): the same shape with the letter d (`LB_NAME=255 node measure-longbase.js <gitbin.js> <temp base> 161 pairs 106`) takes 1.89 to 1.97 s, where short names at the same depth take 1.17 to 1.25 s, names of 255 at 30,200 take 1.09 to 1.16 s, and 540 entries with names of 255 at 3,869 take 1.07 to 1.09 s (first 1.49). So it is slow only where an entry's own path comes within about 100 characters of the 32,767 Windows allows. Which call slows there was not looked for: the cap of RS-A-pathcap refuses the shape whatever the cause, and the item starts from this repro.
  - RS-A-reachpath (Fable F2; CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run; the same on 27e43a4 and older: `d = path.join(wt, n)` is made for every name): a repo at a path of 32,429 characters whose `.git/worktrees` is a junction to a short folder of 2,400 empty files with 255-character names: `''` after 1.31 to 1.53 s, 1.68 to 1.81 s with U+4E2D. Only `top.dir` is counted, not the path the folder is reached by. With `d` made only for folder entries: 0.13 to 0.21 s (Fable).
  - RS-A-pathcap, the fix idea for those three and for RS-A-longgitdir (INFERRED, not tried): the slowest of these need a path far longer than git can open (Git for Windows stops at 4,096 characters with long paths on, Linux at 4,096 bytes, macOS at 1,024). A gitdir or worktrees folder at a longer path could keep git out with a word of its own before anything is counted. Below 4,096 the slowest found is 1.7 s (RS-A-widepath at 3,869).
- Not acted on, by decision: no third term in the count. Each round found a shape about twice as slow as the last one fixed, the last two at paths of thousands of characters and the slowest past what git can open (INFERRED), while an older check next to this one lets 16 s through (RS-A-longgitdir). The cap is the better fix and a change of its own.
- Fable, sound on 2040118 (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run): counting `wt.length` in place of `top.dir.length` fails the test; junction entries to folders at 3,900 characters take 0.70 to 1.03 s; `work.left` stays whole; one entry past the limit inside `far` answers `many-worktrees`; shares from `work.left` 84.6% and 82.8% for the two under cases of 2040118.

## Next version: RS-A-firstcheck (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-firstcheck` off 60c2a4b)

PR #50 (RS-A-nestedone) merged as 60c2a4b at 2026-11-12 00:22:34Z on the user's word, "Yes, merge it", with 9 of 9 jobs passed on its last head 07e4aa2 (CONFIRMED here from `gh`). Round 4 was clean. The merge was a step of its own, after the jobs were read.

- Before that, a macOS job stalled on that head. The cause is not proven. CONFIRMED here from the Actions API, except where labeled:
  - CI run 43113992715, first attempt, job "macos-latest, Node 22" (130839475893): the step "trailmark tests" started at 22:50:44Z on 2026-11-11 and never ended. Auto-merge was taken off and the run cancelled. The job closed as cancelled at 23:35:21Z, 44 minutes 37 seconds into the step. The other 8 jobs had passed.
  - No log exists for that job: the logs call answers 404. After the cancel the step still reads `in_progress`, and the steps after it never ran. The job's annotations are the cancel and GitHub's notice of capacity constraints on macOS arm64 runners.
  - The same step of the same job on other heads: 2 min 38 s on fe29963, 2 min 29 s on 2389955, 2 min 39 s on 39027c2, 1 min 52 s on df2da83. Three reruns of the job on 07e4aa2 (attempts 2 to 4, 23:47Z to 00:07Z): 2 min 26 s, 1 min 5 s and 2 min 18 s, each passed. 07e4aa2 differs from df2da83 in this file only.
  - INFERRED: the runner stopped answering. A step that only hangs still takes the cancel, and the steps after it run and leave a log. A test that froze the whole machine is not ruled out. It gets no "flaky" label and no place on "Load-sensitive tests": it is recorded as unexplained, with the evidence above.
  - The user decided to merge with it recorded so. If a macOS job runs far past its usual time again: take auto-merge off, cancel, ask for the log, compare the step's times, tell the user.
- New item, RS-D-jobtimeout (dev tooling; proposed to the user on 2026-11-11, not answered): `.github/workflows/tests.yml` sets no `timeout-minutes`, so a job that hangs holds a PR for GitHub's default of 6 hours. Fix idea: a limit of about 20 minutes per job, in a PR of its own. Repro: the run above.

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 60c2a4b (Windows): the first thing a hook does is find the repo's memory folder (`hookMemory` in the launcher, then `findMemoryDir` and `repoRoot` in `lib/paths.js`), and `repoRoot` ran the check of the repo's config files with no look at the hook's time. The hook's 5 s for git were counted from the hook's start, so this check's cost came off what `gitFacts` got afterwards, but nothing stopped the check itself. Through the real PreCompact hook (`repro-nestedone-hook.js`, the launcher started as a process and timed from outside): with the session 8 folders down, each folder with a `.git` whose config includes 20 files with a quote, the hook takes 12.3 to 13.5 s today (four rounds of three hooks; 10.05 to 10.30 s when RS-A-nestedone measured it); with no includes 0.87 to 0.96 s. The same check had no limit in the context watch, which runs after every tool call past its first step, in the PreToolUse hook, which Claude Code and Codex stop after 5 s, and in the sweep's lookup of each folder the session committed in.
- Fix:
  - `lib/paths.js`, both copies (the same text but for two comment lines): `repoRoot(cwd, timeoutMs, left)` takes what is left of the caller's time as a function. With it, the check and each git call run inside `withTime(left, fn, 250)`; no git call is started with under 250 ms left; and a git call's timeout is what is left, in whole ms, up to its own (`timeoutMs`). A check that ran out of time, a git call not started and a git call that timed out all end the same way: the root is found from the `.git` entries (`localRepoRoot`), with no git and no config file read. The check `git()` makes right before it starts git answers out of time as a timeout too. `findMemoryDir` hands on `ctx.left`. With no `left` nothing changes. One command does hand a clock in: `write-handoff` with `--session`, through its sweep (round 1).
  - The launchers, each by hand: `hookMemory` takes the clock and gives it to both lookups, the one from the hook's folder and the one from the folder the session started in. `hookMode` hands in 5 s less the time since the hook started, and 3 s for the PreToolUse hook. The sweep's lookup gets its deadline less the 0.8 s it already kept back. Claude Code's copy: the context watch hands in the same 5 s.
  - A decision: the PreToolUse hook names 3 s, not 5. Both hosts stop that hook after 5 s, it holds up the user's command, and it still has to start the writer for its record (a plain hook takes 0.9 s here). With 5 s the check could run for 4.75 s and the record would be lost to the host's limit, as it was before the fix.
- After, CONFIRMED here: the same real hook takes 4.92 to 4.99 s; two nested gitdirs 4.45 s (4.4 s before); no includes 0.90 to 0.95 s. The snapshot then says "Uncommitted files: not read (git-timeout)", since the first check used the 5 s: the hook is on time and says what it couldn't read. The old and the new code were timed in turn (old, new, new, old, three hooks each time; the old code from `git archive 60c2a4b` in a folder of its own).
- A change in behavior: a hook whose first check takes longer than its time now finds the memory folder without git and reads no status. Before, it went on until the host stopped it (10 s, 15 s for PreCompact; 5 s for PreToolUse), and a hook that was stopped wrote nothing.
- Tests:
  - "git pointers: finding the repo for a hook stops with the hook's time (RS-A-firstcheck)", `handoff-checks.test.js`, both copies. Part 1, the two libraries loaded afresh for each case, with stand-ins that note the timeout of each git call and the line each `withTime` call names, and that run every call with 20 s: no clock, and a number in place of one, as before; 5 s, 2000.7 ms, 2 s with git calls of 1 s, NaN, Infinity; 250 ms goes on and 249 ms reads no file and starts no git, and the root is still the repo; short of time from the third file; short after the check; rev-parse stopped; the first rev-parse failing and the second one's time; out of time in the check before git; git kept out; and `findMemoryDir` handing the time on. Part 2, the real launcher with a preload that notes the same calls and stands the clock still, moving it by a set amount when a set config file is read: PreCompact with 3 s gone (2 s for each later file and for the root call) and with 4.8 s gone (no third file, no root call, the snapshot written and saying the time ran out); the PreToolUse hook with 1.5 s gone (1.5 s for each later file) and with 2.8 s gone (no third file, the record written); a hook whose folder is in another repo, where the lookup from the session's own folder gets the 2 s left; session start, where the files of another commit folder's config get 1.7 s each. Round 1 added two session-start cases with the clock moved inside the sweep's lookup.
  - "context watch: finding the memory folder is inside the hook's 5 s for git (RS-A-firstcheck)", `context-watch.test.js`, Claude Code's copy: the same preload, 3 s gone and 4.8 s gone, and the hook still asks.
  - No case runs on real time: the clock stands still in part 2 and every stand-in runs git with time to spare.
- Mutations (`mutate-firstcheck.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-firstcheck'`, a probe caught only with a count of failed tests): the files of 60c2a4b three ways (`paths.js` and the launchers, `paths.js` alone, the launchers alone) and 30 single changes. In `paths.js`: no clock taken; anything handed in counting as a clock; a line of 249 and one of 251; no line named; the check outside the time; out of time answering the folder itself; the git calls outside the time; git started with too little; no git at the line itself; a timeout not in whole ms; git given its own timeout whatever is left; git given what is left past its own timeout; an answer that is no number not told apart; too little time not read as a timeout; the check before git not knowing the time ran out; `findMemoryDir` not handing the time on. In the launchers: each of the two lookups without the clock; no clock handed in; a clock that does not run; the PreToolUse hook with 5 s and with 2 s; every hook with 3 s; the other hooks with 4 s; the sweep's lookup without a clock, and keeping nothing back; and three for the context watch (no clock handed in, not passed on, 4 s). All 33 fail: a change to both copies in at least 2 of the 3 tests, a change to the context watch in 1. Unchanged passes 3 of 3. One whole run, with nothing to run again, on Node 26.13.1.
- Docs: SECURITY.md, the last sentences of the bullet on includes. MANUAL.md is unchanged: no new reason word reaches a user.
- Not shown: macOS and Linux times; a single git call that hangs in the first check (its timeout is what is left, as the stand-in shows, but no real hung git was run); Codex's hooks through Codex itself.
- Deferred: nothing new from the fix. RS-A-runleft stands (the same shape in `gitfacts.js`'s runner).

## Next version: RS-A-nestedone (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-nestedone` off fe29963, merged in PR #50 as 60c2a4b)

PR #49 (RS-A-linkfolders) merged as fe29963 at 2026-11-11 19:11:26Z by auto-merge, after 9 of 9 jobs passed on its last head 045368a (CONFIRMED here from `gh`). Auto-merge was turned on as a step of its own, once that head's 9 jobs were listed. Its round 2 (Fable, on f0791a5..045368a): "Nothing CONFIRMED wrong in the changed test". CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: in the three let-through cases the clock goes back at the lstat of `a`, before `ls-files`, `check-attr` and `status` (timeout 4000, was 900 with the old stub); the test passes with its temp folder under `d7`, `a` or `f`, in other spellings on Windows, through a junction, and on Linux (WSL); it fails on d212f16's library.

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on fe29963 (Windows): the checks of a repo's config files never looked at the hook's time for git. A config file with a quote in it is read by git itself (a call each, with a timeout of 3 s each), 50 files are allowed, and a file can be 8 MB. PR #47 put a look at the time before each nested repo's check, not inside one.
  - Through the library (`repro-nestedone.js` and `repro-nestedone-more.js`, both timing `gitFacts(..., ['status'])`): a config that includes 48 files with a quote, with 2 s given: 3.13 s (in a tracked file turned folder with a `.git`) and 2.89 s (the session repo's own). With 5 s given: 8 folders above the session's folder, each with a `.git` that includes 20 such files, 10.0 to 10.2 s; the repo's config including 24 plain files of 4 MB, 6.1 s.
  - Through the real PreCompact hook (`repro-nestedone-hook.js`, the launcher started as a process and timed from outside): no includes 0.75 s; 48 such files in one nested gitdir 3.7 s, inside the 5 s; two such nested gitdirs (two tracked files turned folders, each with a `.git` file to a gitdir whose config includes the 48 files) 6.4 s.
- Fix, both copies:
  - `gitbin.js`: `withTime(left, fn)`, exported, sets what is left of the caller's time while `fn` runs. `farConfig` answers `git-timeout` before it reads a file when under 800 ms are left. `configEntries` starts git with a timeout of what is left, at most 3 s. `farGitConfigIn` doesn't keep a `git-timeout` answer for the next call. A call with no `withTime` around it is unchanged (the launcher's `paths.js`).
  - `gitfacts.js`: the first check in `gitFacts`, the check before each git call (`r.run`), both `farNested` calls and `farModules` run inside `withTime(r.left, ...)`. A check that ran out of time reads as a git call that did: `r.late`, and `git-timeout` for status. It is not `gitRefused` and not `submodule-pointer`, so no hook says git is kept out of the repo because of it.
- After, CONFIRMED here: through the library with 2 s given, 48 files: 1.21 s and 1.25 s, `git-timeout`. With 5 s given, 48 files: 3.3 s, status read, as before; the 8 folders above: 4.25 s; the 24 files of 4 MB: 4.33 s. Through the real hook: two nested gitdirs 4.4 s; one, 3.7 s as before.
- Not reached by this fix, CONFIRMED here: a hook's first check (`hookMemory` in the launcher, to find the memory folder) calls `farGitConfig` with no time, and its answer is kept for the session's folder. The real hook with the 8 folders above the session's folder takes 10.05 to 10.30 s on fe29963 and the same with the fix. New item RS-A-firstcheck, below.
- A change in behavior: with under 800 ms left when a config file is reached, the check stops and status isn't read (`git-timeout`); before, it went on. A hook with time in hand answers as before.
- Test: "git pointers: the caller's time is looked at before each config file is read (RS-A-nestedone)", both copies, every system, with six included files that hold a quote.
  - The library, with what is left handed in as a scripted function: time in hand `''`; none from the start `git-timeout`; short at the fifth ask only, which is the look before the third file, `git-timeout`; the same repo afterwards with no limit `''`, so the answer wasn't kept; on a plain repo 900 ms `''` and 700 ms `git-timeout`.
  - The library loaded again with a stand-in for `execFileSync` that notes the timeout of each `--file` call: seven of 2000 with 2 s left, seven of 3000 with no limit.
  - `gitFacts` with `Date.now` standing still, then jumping to 500 ms before the end at the lstat of the second included file (matched on the path from the test's own folder): the session's repo, a tracked file turned folder, a submodule with a `.git` file, a gitdir in `.git/modules`, and a session in a subfolder, where the jump comes the second time through. Each gives `git-timeout` for status and no `gitRefused`, and the last of the six files is not looked at after the jump.
  - Fails on fe29963.
- Mutations (`mutate-nestedone.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-nestedone'`): the parent's two files and 16 single changes. No look; 400 for 800; 1000 for 800; the look only before the first file; git started with 3 s whatever is left; a `git-timeout` answer kept; `withTime` setting nothing; each of the five `withTime` calls in `gitfacts.js` taken out; the first check out of time read as git kept out; the check before a git call not told apart; a nested folder out of time read as `submodule-pointer`; the same for a submodule. All 17 fail; unchanged passes. The first run had one survivor, the look only before the first file: `config.worktree` is looked for after the included files and noticed the time there. The case that is short at one ask only closed it.
- Docs: SECURITY.md, the bullet on includes and one sentence in the paragraph on the status check. MANUAL.md is unchanged: no new reason word reaches a user.
- Not shown: macOS and Linux times; the snapshot's words in the real hook when the time runs out (the library's answer is tested, and the hook's wording code is unchanged); a single file that git takes long to read (its timeout is what is left).
- Deferred, older, the same on fe29963 and with the fix:
  - New, RS-A-firstcheck, FLAGGED for the time, CONFIRMED here: a hook's first check has no look at the time (above). Repro: `node repro-nestedone-hook.js <root> <temp folder> 48 strays` times the PreCompact hook at 10.2 s. Fix idea: the launcher hands the hook's time to that check with `withTime`, and `repoRoot` then finds the root without git, as it does for a git that timed out. What `localRepoRoot` does once the time has run out needs reading first.
  - RS-A-lfsconfigtime: its note that `farGitConfig(cwd)` at the start of `gitFacts` has no look at the time is settled by this fix. The `.lfsconfig` part stays.

## Next version: RS-A-linkfolders (2026-11-11, branch `claude/rs-a-linkfolders` off d212f16, merged in PR #49 as fe29963)

PR #48 (RS-A-abovegit) merged as d212f16 at 2026-11-11 18:26:42Z by auto-merge, after 9 of 9 jobs passed on its last head b7d3d37 (CONFIRMED here from `gh`). Auto-merge was turned on as a step of its own, once `gh pr view` showed b7d3d37 with its 9 jobs listed; right after the push it showed none for some seconds. The merge was the session's to make under the user's delegation of 2026-11-11 (round 1 found nothing CONFIRMED wrong in the changed code, round 2 found the reworded sentences true).

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on d212f16 (Windows, `repro-linkfolders.js`; first seen by Fable in the review of PR #47): before `git status`, the first loop of `farWorktree` (`gitfacts.js`) goes through every folder on the way to a tracked file, with an lstat for each and, for a link, a readlink and a walk of where it leads, and it never looked at the time. With 5 s given and N tracked files `dN/f` where each `dN` is a junction to a folder at the end of a 900-character path: N=100 1.41 s (status read), N=250 2.94 s (status read), N=500 5.29 s, N=1000 10.51 s, the last two `git-timeout` from a later look. About 10 ms for each link; 20,000 folders are allowed. 500 plain folders: 0.44 s.
- Fix (`gitfacts.js`, both copies, one line at the top of that loop): `if (r.left() < 800) return 'git-timeout';`, as the two loops below it have. It comes before every folder, a plain one too: the look is one `Date.now()`, and what a folder is isn't known before its lstat.
- After, CONFIRMED here: N=100 1.40 s and N=250 3.02 s (status read), N=500 and N=1000 both 4.21 s (`git-timeout`); 500 plain folders 0.45 s. The most past the time is one folder's check.
- A change in behavior: with 250 to 799 ms left when a folder is reached, the old code went on; the new answers `git-timeout`. The loops over folders with a `.git` and over submodules have the same rule.
- Test: "git status: the time is looked at before each folder of tracked files (RS-A-linkfolders)", both copies, every system: four folders with a tracked file each, then each a link to one plain folder. `Date.now` stands still and jumps to 500 or 900 ms before the end of the 5 s at the first look at one of the four; the folders looked at are counted, no seconds are measured. 500 ms left: `git-timeout` and one folder looked at. 900 ms left, and time in hand: all four, status read. The same two cases with plain folders. Fails on d212f16.
- Mutations (`mutate-linkfolders.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-linkfolders'`): the parent's file; 400 for 800; 1000 for 800; the time looked at once in 500 folders; the time looked at for links only; another reason word. All 6 fail; unchanged passes.
- Docs: one sentence in SECURITY.md's paragraph on the status check.
- Not shown: macOS and Linux times; what one link's walk can cost at most (the walk has the limits of `localPath`, read, not measured).

Nothing on disk is needed, only an index (`git update-index --index-info`): 500 files in one folder 1,000 deep take 1.6 s before the first folder is looked at; 1,500 files 1,500 deep take 11.8 s with 5 s given. Not run here either: it needs about 25,000 folders, which stall this drive (LESSONS 2026-11-10).
  - RS-A-biglisting, other numbers: 499 links, three names, one folder of 20,000 files took 4.3 to 6.2 s on both versions (8.0 s is listed).

- What was wrong, CONFIRMED here on 8fd6b84 (Windows, `repro-abovegit.js`): git goes on up past a `.git` it doesn't accept, so `farGitConfigIn` checks each one it finds between the session's folder and the repo, and each got a whole `farGit` with a limit on the work of its own. A repo whose config names two `lfs.storage` folders of 120 links to a folder at the end of a 900-character path, and N folders below it, one inside the other, each with a `.git` folder holding only such a config (no HEAD), checked from the innermost: N=0 `''` after 1.03 s, N=1 `''` 2.03 s, N=3 `''` 4.80 s, N=8 `''` 10.54 s. About 1 s for each; 512 levels are allowed.
- Fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): `farGitConfigIn` makes one `{ left: MAX_PATH_WORK }` for each way up (the folder as given, and its real path where that differs) and hands it to every `farGit` on that way, and through `farBareProbe` to the `farGit` an older git's bare repo gets. `farGit` takes it as the option `work`, and makes its own when given none (`farNested`, `farModules`, `localRepoRoot`: unchanged).
  - One limit for each way up, not one for both: where the session's folder is given in another spelling than the disk has, the same repo is checked on both ways, and a repo that counts over half the limit (2,000 submodule gitdirs can count 6 million) would be refused for the spelling alone. The most that is let through is then two limits, about 2 s.
- After, CONFIRMED here: N=0 `''` 1.03 s; N=1, 3 and 8 `many-config-paths` after 1.29, 1.55 and 1.59 s.
- A change in behavior: a session folder below a `.git` that git doesn't accept, where that `.git` and the repo above it count over 8 million between them, was let through and is now refused. An ordinary stray `.git` takes next to nothing from the limit of 8,388,608, CONFIRMED here (`probe-abovegit-count.js`, Windows): a file that isn't a pointer counts 0, a pointer to a gitdir that is gone 120, an empty folder 400, a folder with a plain config 410, and a fresh `git init` repo 390.
- Test: "git pointers: every .git found on one way up from the session's folder shares one limit on the work (RS-A-abovegit)", both copies, every system. Stores of 120 long links, two fit in the limit and three don't. Let through: a repo naming two; the same with two `.git` folders below it that name none; one in the repo and one in a `.git` below. Refused: two and one; one and one and one; none in the repo and one in each of three below. A linked session folder whose two ways up each end at a repo with two stores is let through. With a git that says it is 2.34.1 (the library loaded again with a stand-in for `execFileSync`, in the test's own process), a bare repo naming one store below a repo naming one is let through, and one naming two is refused. Fails on 8fd6b84.
- Mutations (`mutate-abovegit.js`, each through `--only 'RS-A-abovegit'`): the parent's file; a `.git` on the way up checked with a limit of its own; the bare repo probe not given the limit; the probe not passing it on; `farGit` taking no limit from its caller; one limit for both ways up; the limit doubled; the limit halved. All 8 fail; unchanged passes.

- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies):

Once the walks before held that many, the next alternate answers `many-entries`. Plain folders cost listings, which the limit on the work doesn't count.

- After the fix, CONFIRMED here (the same script): A `many-config-paths` in 1.68 s, B `many-config-paths` in 1.33 s, C `many-entries` in 2.31 s. ## Next version: RS-A-manylinks (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-manylinks` off b3e7047, merged in PR #42 as cc06828)

Deferred from RS-A-foldkeys' review, and FLAGGED in PR #41's round 1 for the largest times seen. Taken next on my recommendation; the user: "Yes, continue".

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on b3e7047 (Windows, drive C:, junctions; `repro-manylinks.js` in the session's scratchpad): a repo whose `.git/zz` holds 3,000 links, each to a folder of its own 40 levels down: 9.2 s, `''`. With 9,999 links to folders two levels down: 5.4 s, `''`. The walks' budgets count entries, and an entry that is a link costs what its target's path does. An `lfs.storage` store of those shapes was already refused (`many-config-paths`, 2.2 to 3.0 s), since that walk runs inside the limit on a config's path values. CONFIRMED by Fable in PR #41's round 1, not re-run: three stores next to alternates, each of 1,000 symlinks to a chain of 37 links, 57.9 s in WSL.
- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): `linkedEntries` now wraps its walk (`linkedEntriesIn`) in a limit on the work `localPath` does for the links it follows, `MAX_LINK_WORK`, 4 MiB counted as the limit on path values counts (`WALK_LEFT`). Inside a check of config values the smaller of the two holds, and what the links cost is taken off that check's own limit. A link that can't be followed because the work ran out answers `many-entries`; one that leads to a share or loops answers `linked-gitdir` as before.
- Why 4 MiB: 3,000 links to nearby folders were accepted before (1.3 s) and stay accepted (1.5 s here); half the limit would refuse them. About 3,500 such links fit, or about 350 to folders 40 levels down.
- After the fix, CONFIRMED here (the same script): both slow shapes answer `many-entries` in 1.3 to 1.8 s, in `.git/zz` and as a store. 12, 300 and 3,000 links to folders two levels down answer `''` in 16 ms, 0.14 s and 1.5 s.
- Test: "git pointers: links in a gitdir or a store are followed up to a limit on the work, then git is kept out (RS-A-manylinks)", both copies, with junctions (symlinks off Windows) that all lead to one folder at the end of a path 900 characters long (about 400 parts named `d`). A link costs what walking its target's path does, each part by the length of the path up to it, so such a link counts about 27,000 of the 4 MiB with no folder of its own. 12 and 125 links in a gitdir pass, 220 give `many-entries`; links that loop give `linked-gitdir`; a gitdir and a store next to its alternate with 125 each pass (the limit is one walk's); a store of 125 passes and one of 220 doesn't; two stores of 125 pass and three give `many-config-paths`. It fails with b3e7047's `gitbin.js` in both copies, and takes 12 to 13 s in each (most of it the checks' own counted work, about 0.8 s for a walk at the limit).
  - Margins, CONFIRMED here (`probe-longpath.js`, scratchpad): the most links accepted is 149 on Windows (junctions, base path 28 characters) and 149 in WSL (symlinks, Node 18, base 22). A longer temp path means fewer parts and a few more links: INFERRED 150 to 153 for the temp paths of the check script and macOS (round 1's replay of the counting). 125 is over half of either and 220 under twice either, so the halved and doubled limits both fail the test.
  - The first versions gave each link a folder of its own 40 levels down, 33,600 folders in all. That test passed in 12 to 24 s, but three runs took 481, 961 and 1,308 s. CONFIRMED here, not the code under test: during the 481 s both test processes had no CPU time and no I/O count for 7 minutes (a sampler every 15 s, `sample-procs.ps1`), `rm` calls of the check script hung the same way, and Windows logged ReFS event 147 for `rm.exe` on E:, an I/O of 373 s. Which ReFS work stalls is not known; the test no longer makes those folders (LESSONS, 2026-11-10).

## Next version: RS-A-dottracked (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-dottracked` off b53b065, merged in PR #40 as 06bfe98)

Deferred from PR #39's review and flagged as serious (access, Windows). Taken next on my recommendation under the user's standing word of 2026-11-10 to work the deferred items one at a time ("continue").

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on b53b065 (Windows, Git 2.56.0.windows.4; `repro-dottracked.js` and `repro-dottracked3.js` with `repro-dottracked3.sh` in the session's scratchpad). `gitfacts.js` `farWorktree` looked up a tracked name as written. Node opens `d.` literally and finds nothing, so the entry was passed over:
  - A tracked file `s.`, `s ` or `s..`, with a folder `s` on disk that holds a `.git` pointing at a share: `''`. With the file tracked as `s`: `submodule-pointer`.
  - What git opens, from `git status` on an index made in WSL, with the tracked content on disk under the plain names. Tracked `d./f` and `e /f`: found (git opens `d/f` and `e/f`), with every setting tried. Tracked file `u.`: not found by default, found with `core.fscache=false`, which a repo's own config can set. `k/f`, the control: found.
  - Not a fault: a gitlink `m.` or `x./m` was already refused on b53b065 (`farNested` goes through `localPath`, which has the rule since PR #39). Names with a stream's name after them (`s::$INDEX_ALLOCATION/f`, `t::$DATA`, `v:$I30:$INDEX_ALLOCATION/f`) are not found by git with `core.protectNTFS` and `core.fscache` each on and off (`repro-dottracked4.js`), so they get no rule. That was run on the Dev Drive (ReFS); plain NTFS was not tried.
  - That a link `d` to a share would be followed for a tracked `d./f` is INFERRED: making such a link needs a right this session doesn't have (EPERM), and a junction can't name a share.
- The fix: on Windows a tracked path with a name that ends in a dot or a space keeps status from being read (`odd-path`). It is `gitbin.js`'s `dotEnd`, now exported, called once per index entry in `farWorktree`. Elsewhere `dotEnd` is always false, so nothing changes there. The cost: a repo on Windows whose index holds such a name (one that can't be checked out there with git's defaults) gets no file list in its snapshot.
- Test: "git status: a tracked name that ends in a dot or a space keeps status unread on Windows (RS-A-dottracked)", both copies. It passes with the fix and fails with b53b065's `gitfacts.js` at its first dotted name (`undefined` for `odd-path`). Run on Windows only so far.
- Docs: MANUAL.md's `odd-path` row for the file list and one sentence in SECURITY.md. `SHA256SUMS` regenerated.
- Round 1, on b53b065..8ef8f24 (PR #40). Fable on the code (65 repo pairs on a ReFS and an NTFS drive, indexes made in WSL or written raw, real `git status` five ways), Sonnet on the docs. Nothing CONFIRMED in the changed code. CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: the rule returns before every use of a tracked name; no repo without such a name changed its answer; in WSL nine names answer as before; stream names, 8.3 names (NTFS), device names, letter case and 300-character paths show no mismatch between git and the check.
  - Three older holes of the same class, in the same loop, each CONFIRMED here on 8ef8f24 (`repro-dott-r1.js`, scratchpad; it writes the index as a file) and fixed in the batch after it, pulled in without the user's word for it as part of this item. Windows git takes a backslash in a tracked name as a separator and finds `d\s`, `x/d\f` and `E:d/f` under the plain disk path.
    - A tracked `d\s` with three more names in the root, `d/s` on disk holding a `.git` that points at a share: `''`. The listing branch looked for a folder named `d\s`. With `d\s` alone: `submodule-pointer`.
    - A tracked `d\f` or `x/d\f` with `d` a junction to a folder the check refuses: `''`, where `d/f` gives `linked-folder`. The folders on the way were split on `/` alone.
    - A tracked `E:d/f` with `d` such a junction: `filter-check` here (Fable, with `check-attr` stood in for, saw status read). No rule refused it.
  - The fix: on Windows a tracked path with a backslash, or with a drive letter and a colon at its start, gives `odd-path`, next to the dot rule. A colon elsewhere is left alone. The cost is the same as the dot rule's: no file list on Windows for a repo with such a name, which git there refuses to check out.
  - Test: the same test, with an index written as a file for names Windows git won't add. It now covers gitlinks and both separators, the two gaps Fable named. Single changes to the rule (`mutate-dott.js`, `mutate-dott2.js`): the dot rule alone (8ef8f24's), no backslash part, no drive letter part, no dot rule, a colon anywhere, and a drive letter after any `/` each fail it. A backslash refused on every system passes on Windows and can only fail elsewhere: left to CI.

Each CONFIRMED here; those fourteen and one more now name their section (PLAYBOOK, Writing code and docs, step 10). ## Next version: RS-A-foldkeys and RS-A-trailingdot (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-foldkeys` off f97553a, merged in PR #39 as b53b065)

Both were deferred from PR #38's reviews and flagged as serious: they are about access (a folder or file the check never looks at), on Windows. The user pulled them in on 2026-11-10.

- RS-A-foldkeys, the fault, CONFIRMED here on f97553a (Windows, `repro-fold.js` in the session's scratchpad; each case below is also in the test). Four sets of "already seen" (five keyed places, since one set is keyed at two) were keyed by `toLowerCase()` on Windows, and JavaScript lower-cases `k` and the Kelvin sign (U+212A) alike while NTFS and ReFS keep them as two names. With the look-alike named first, the second was skipped and the answer was `''`:
  - `farConfig`'s set of config files read: `.git/config` including `k.cfg`, then `<U+212A>.cfg` whose `core.attributesFile` names a share. Alone: `network-config`.
  - `linkedEntries`' set of folders walked: `.git/refs/heads/k` next to `.git/refs/heads/<U+212A>` holding a junction loop. Alone: `linked-gitdir`.
  - `moduleGitdirs`' set, twice: `.git/modules/k` next to a gitdir `.git/modules/<U+212A>` whose config names a share (`farModules`), and the same for the `objects` folder of two such gitdirs.
  - `farGitConfig`'s set of folders searched for a `.git`: the session in `w/k/link`, a junction to `w/<U+212A>/sub`, where `w/k` is a repo whose config names a share. From a plain folder there: `network-config`.
  - The review named the second and guessed at the third and fourth; the first and the last are new, found by searching for the pattern.
- The fix: each set is keyed by the exact text. A config file named again in another case is read again, within the 50 files. A folder a link leads to is told by the system's own path for it (`onDisk`, rounds 1 to 3 below), so the walks' sets know it under one text.
- RS-A-trailingdot, the fault, CONFIRMED here on f97553a: Windows programs drop dots and spaces from a name's end, and Node opens the name as written. `cmd /c dir k.` lists `k`; `k./a` reaches `k/a`. With `lfs.storage = <path>/k.`, git-lfs 3.8.1 reports `LfsStorageDir=.../k.` and `git add` of a tracked file writes the object into `k/objects/...` (run here). `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` run with the folder `k.` answers `.../k` (run here). The check answered `''` for `lfs.storage = k.`, `k..`, `k./a` (the folder `k` holding a junction loop) and for a session folder given as `k.` (the repo `k` naming a share in its config).
  - Not a fault: a `.git` file with `gitdir: ../real.`. git itself answers "gitfile does not point to a valid repository" (run here).
- The fix: `dotEnd(p)`, Windows only: a path with a name that ends in a dot or a space, `.` and `..` apart. It is refused in `oddRelative` (a value, an include, an alternates line, a pointer, a link's target: `odd-config-path` or the caller's reason), in `farGitConfig` for the session folder and `farNested` for a nested repo's (`odd-path`), and in `localPath` itself (never a local path, whoever asks). Since round 1 (below) one caller is left out: values under `alias` and `pretty` (`WORDS_KEY`), where `farPathValue` asks `driveRelative` in place of `oddRelative` and passes `words` to `localPath`. Quoted values (`storage = "k "`) are decoded before the check and refused too (run here through a real config file).
- The test: "git pointers: two names JavaScript lower-cases alike are two names, and a name Windows opens under another is refused (next version)". It fails on f97553a's `gitbin.js` in both copies. 16 single changes of the fix each fail it: 15 on Windows, and the Windows-only switch of `dotEnd` in WSL (where `k.` must stay a name like any other). The list is `muts-fold.json` in the scratchpad, not kept in the repo. One junction is made, on Windows only, with `busyRetry`.
- What a user can meet: on Windows, a config value, include, pointer or session folder with a name that ends in a dot or a space is now refused. For a file's or folder's name that was INFERRED never in a real repo (Windows programs can't make or open such a name); round 1 showed it wrong for values that are not paths, and aliases and log formats are now left out (below). Still refused: a path setting, or a key of one's own that ends like one, whose value has a word ending in a dot or a space at its end or before a slash. A file system that folds `k` and the Kelvin sign (macOS's) has one folder for the two, which the checks then read once for each text.
- Seen and left, older, same pattern in two comparisons (INFERRED from the code on f97553a and 3e1ff9f, not run): `shareRoot` lower-cases a share's server and name before comparing it with the session's own share, so `//srv/<U+212A>` would count as the session's `//srv/k` if a server keeps the two apart; and `sameDir` lower-cases when it passes over a PATH entry equal to the session folder, which errs toward passing over. Deferred as RS-A-sharefold. Repro idea: a session on `//srv/k` and a value naming `//srv/<U+212A>/x`, through `farGitConfig`; needs a server that has both shares.
- Not run: macOS locally, the two long suites (CI), a real hook, NTFS (this machine's temp drive is ReFS; the system drive's temp folder, NTFS, ran the repro script).
- Round 1, on f97553a..3e1ff9f (PR #39). Fable (Windows and WSL, exported copies) on the code, Sonnet on the docs. ## Next version: RS-A-storagewalk (2026-11-10, branch `claude/rs-a-storagewalk` off afe9bf9, merged in PR #38 as f97553a)

The user pulled it in on 2026-11-10 ("Yes, fix storagewalk"), after PR #37 merged.

- The fault, CONFIRMED here on afe9bf9 (Windows): `lfs.storage` in 200 spellings of one folder with 10,100 entries through `farLfsEntries` is accepted in 2.7 s, with 200 `statSync` and 200 `readdirSync` calls. 52,000 spellings of an empty folder (16 parts of `./` or `.//` each) are refused after 4.9 s and 45,636 calls of each. `farPathValue` kept its set of walked folders for one value only, and the work limit doesn't count a store's walk.
- The fix (`gitbin.js`, both copies): the folders `lfs.storage` values led to are kept for one check (`STORES`, set by `limitedWalk` like the lookup cache), by the exact path `localPath` found (lower-cased on Windows in 3dbc23c, which round 1 showed wrong; see below). A folder or file already seen under the same path text costs no disk call. Two limits bound what that leaves: more than 64 different folders (`MAX_LFS_STORES`), and no further folder once those before it held 20,000 looked-at entries between them (`MAX_STORE_ENTRIES`; `linkedEntries` reports the count through `tally`). Past either the answer is `many-config-paths`.
- Why two limits: the first version had only the 64. Folders of files are cheap (64 of 12,000 files: 1.3 s), but 64 stores laid out as git-lfs does it (`objects/ab/cd`, 10,100 folders each) were accepted in 45 s, CONFIRMED here before the commit. One such store takes 0.65 to 0.8 s on Windows: that is older behavior (the store budget of 10,000 entries), not changed here.
- After the fix, CONFIRMED here: the 200 spellings take 22 ms on Windows and 18 ms in WSL with one call of each. The 52,000 spellings are refused in 0.46 s (WSL 0.23 s), by the work limit. Nested stores on Windows: one 0.8 s, two 1.4 s (accepted), a third after two of 10,000 refused at 1.6 to 1.7 s (WSL 0.15 s). The most accepted, three nested stores of 9,997, 10,000 and 10,000 looked-at entries: 2.3 to 2.4 s on Windows, 0.23 to 0.27 s in WSL (`probe-sw-worst3.js`). That is above the "1 to 2 seconds" SECURITY.md gives for the work limit's slowest inputs, and SECURITY.md says so for the stores. A limit of 10,000 would bring it to about 1.6 s but refuse any second store after a full one; kept at 20,000 (a judgment, for the user to overrule).
- The test: in "git pointers: many path values are checked once each, and too many are refused (next version)", counted calls of `fs.statSync` and `fs.readdirSync`. It fails on afe9bf9's `gitbin.js` in both copies (`list: 200` for 1). 20 single changes of the fix: 17 fail the test (16 on Windows, "a store's answer is dropped" in WSL only, where links can be made; the key's case on Windows only, since round 1 the lower-casing put back). The other three give the same answers as the fix: the cap answering `''` after setting the limit below 0, and the store set kept or not put back after a check (each shows only together with the other). The list is `muts-sw.json` in the session's scratchpad, not kept in the repo.
- Round 1, on afe9bf9..3dbc23c (PR #38). Fable (Windows and WSL, exported copies) on the code, Sonnet on the docs.
  - One regression in the changed code, CONFIRMED here (in a folder `w`: folders `k` and U+212A, the second holding a junction loop, made as `<U+212A>/a/l1` pointing at `<U+212A>/b` and `<U+212A>/b` pointing back at `<U+212A>/a/l1`, both with `fs.symlinkSync(target, link, 'junction')`; `farLfsEntries` with both values answered `linked-gitdir` on afe9bf9 and `''` on 3dbc23c). The store key was `real.toLowerCase()` on Windows, and JavaScript lower-cases more than NTFS and ReFS fold, so the second folder went unwalked. Fixed in the batch after 3dbc23c: the key is the exact text. A folder written in another case is then walked again, within the two limits. The test lists both folders (it fails with the lower-casing put back: `1 !== 2`); the `s0`/`S0` assertion that pinned the lower-casing is gone.
  - Nothing else differed between old and new: 1,440 cases on Windows and 1,800 in WSL (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run). No false refusal found beyond the stated one. 16 single changes each failed the assertion that names them, and the three equivalent ones are unobservable because `farPathValue` runs only inside `limitedWalk` (CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run).
  - Times, CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run: three stores with 3,000 links to outside folders each are accepted in 1.3 to 2.5 s on Windows and 0.41 to 0.49 s in WSL (0.44 to 0.89 s through `farGitConfig`). The 200 spellings of a 10,000-folder store took 36.8 s on afe9bf9 (WSL 17.3 s), 0.18 s now. SECURITY.md and the code comment give "up to 2.5 seconds on Windows and 0.9 on Linux".
  - Docs findings (Sonnet), all applied: the 64 counts existing folders only, "1 to 2 seconds" stood next to the new 2.4, the store limits are per repo checked, a three-part list in MANUAL.md's row, three stale lines here, and a repro that pointed at a scratchpad.

Fix idea: exact text there too.
    - RS-A-altstores, flagged (time): the LFS store next to an alternate (`<alt>/../lfs`) has no set and no limit, and is walked twice for each line. `.git/objects/info/alternates` with the relative lines `x/0` to `x/19` (git reads them from `.git/objects`, so each line's `../lfs` is `.git/objects/x/lfs`) and `.git/objects/x/lfs` holding 10,000 folders, seen on afe9bf9 and 3dbc23c alike: 410,066 listings, 7.8 s, answer `''`. A 64 KB file holds about 16,000 such lines (INFERRED over an hour, not run).
    - RS-A-biglisting (time): a folder's listing is read whole before the budget counts it. Seen on afe9bf9 and 3dbc23c alike: a store (an `lfs.storage` folder with 9,999 entries before it) ending in one folder of 300,000 files adds 0.54 s on Windows and 0.22 s in WSL while counting 10,000.

The test expected one listing there. It now expects two on every system. Not run here: macOS (CI is the check).
- Round 2 code (Fable, 3dbc23c..585e5ef, Windows and WSL, exported copies): nothing CONFIRMED wrong in the changed `gitbin.js` lines. Its one fault in the changed lines is the macOS test failure above, already fixed in e60807f the way it recommends. CONFIRMED by Fable, not re-run:
  - No input that afe9bf9 refuses and 585e5ef accepts: 267,200 checks through `farLfsEntries` on Windows (8 pairs of names that lower-case alike, 20 spellings each, singles, ordered pairs and 8,000 random lists) and 12,474 in WSL. 3dbc23c accepted 3,120 of them that afe9bf9 refuses; 585e5ef refuses all of those. Not in that comparison: `farGitConfig`, a real share, 8.3 names.
  - The exact key's cost is bounded by the two limits in every case built. Slowest accepted found: three stores of about 10,000 folders, 0.67 to 0.72 s on its drive (which walks a store in 0.2 s, where 0.65 to 0.8 s was measured here, so its times read low). Slowest refused: 0.52 to 0.62 s.
  - Newly refused since 3dbc23c, by design: 65 case spellings of one folder, or three case spellings of a store with 10,000 entries or more. INFERRED unlikely in a real repo. An absolute `core.worktree` in another case doubles a relative store's walk and is still accepted.

  - Deferred from round 2, older behavior (afe9bf9, 3dbc23c and 585e5ef alike), FLAGGED with RS-A-foldkeys as the same class: RS-A-trailingdot (fixed since, section RS-A-foldkeys). On Windows, `lfs.storage = k.` where the folder `k` holds a link the check refuses: the answer is `''`. Node gives ENOENT for `k.`, while Windows programs open `k` (`cmd /c dir k.` lists it, and `git lfs pointer --file=hfile.` with git-lfs 3.8.1 reads `hfile`). INFERRED: git-lfs would use `k` as its store. Not checked: git-lfs given that value as `lfs.storage`, NTFS (seen on ReFS). Repro: folder `k` with the junction loop of round 1 in it, then `farLfsEntries([['lfs.storage', 'k.']], w)`.
- Round 2 docs (Sonnet, 3dbc23c..585e5ef): SECURITY.md said a folder is looked into once "however many values spell it", which the exact-text key made untrue for another case. Reworded, and three repros here made complete. No style findings.
- Round 3 (Sonnet, 585e5ef..9f601cf: one test assertion, one SECURITY.md sentence, dev notes; no `gitbin.js` line, so no security row): nothing CONFIRMED wrong. Stop rule met. One optional INFERRED wording point left as it is: on macOS the second text is a name the file system folds, not strictly "another case".
- What a user can meet: a repo whose `lfs.storage` values lead to three existing folders, the first two with 10,000 entries or more, is now refused (`many-config-paths`). INFERRED rare: git-lfs uses one value, and a relative one exists from one or two of the folders it is tried from.
